Gn 1:27 reads *Et creavit Deus hominem ad imaginem suam*, "And God created man to his own image," without *et similitudinem*.]

[n: (GREG. Moral. lib. II, c. 5.)] « Come, » etc. It is said to those who cleave to God, « Come, » because never to decrease from the divine contemplation but always to increase, and never to withdraw in heart, is, by a steadfast motion, always to be coming. The angels ascend by beholding the Creator, they descend by pressing down, with the sifting of strictness, the creature that unlawfully lifts itself up. To say therefore, « Let us go down and confound their tongue, » is to show within himself what is rightly done, and, by the force of the inward vision, to breathe with hidden motions into their minds the judgments that are to be displayed.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « He divided them. » [n: (ALC.)] In this division of tongues God made nothing new, but divided the [0115B] modes of speaking and the forms of speech among the various nations. Whence we find the same syllables and letters of the same force joined otherwise in different tongues, and often the same nouns or verbs signifying something else; whence, where it is said in Psalm II: « With a rod of iron, » in the Greek there stands ἐν ραβδῳ σιδηρᾶ, *in raudo sidera.* Therefore in Latin *sidera* does not signify *ferrea*, as it does in the Greek, but *astra.*

[n: (AUG. lib. XVI de Civit. c. 4.)] Rightly is the evil affection punished, even that upon which no effect follows. But of what kind was the manner of the punishment? For since the dominion of him who commands lies in the tongue, pride was condemned there, so that he who commanded should not be understood by man, the man who would not understand so as to obey God who commanded.

VERS. 10-25.-- « Sem was a hundred years old. » [0115C] [n: (BEDA, Hexaem., lib. III, t. II, col. 131.)] A great question arises for us according to the letter, how Sem is said to be a hundred years old two years after the flood, when above he is asserted to have been born in the five hundredth year of Noah, and the flood is read to have come in the six hundredth year of the same Noah, etc., *as far as* « because God took him. »

VERS. 26-30.-- « Thare begot Abram and Nachor. » [n: (AUG. quaest. 25 in Gen.)] Not at the same time, but in their own times, from the point at which he began to beget. Nor is the order of birth to be regarded in these, but the signification of the dignity to come, in which Abram excels.

VERS. 31-32.-- « In Ur of the Chaldees. » [n: (HIERON.)] In the Hebrew *in Ur Chesdim* ( )[ed: Migne prints the Hebrew word inside these parentheses; our digitized source has lost it and leaves them empty.], that is, *in the fire of the Chaldees.* Here the Hebrews tell the tale that Abram was cast into [0115D] the fire because he would not adore the fire which the Chaldees worship, and, delivered by God's help, escaped out of the fire of idolatry. Whence it is said to him: « I am he that brought thee out of Ur of the Chaldees. »[var: Vulg. Gn 15:7 reads *Ego Dominus qui eduxi te de Ur Chaldaeorum*, "I am the Lord who brought thee out of Ur of the Chaldees."]

« Aram the father of Melcha. »[var: Vulg. Gn 11:29 reads *Aran patris Melchae*; Migne prints the name *Aram* here and again in the gloss below.] [n: (Ibid.)] The same is the father of both. Aram, son of Thare, brother of Abraham and of Nachor, begot two daughters, Melcha and Sarai, surnamed Jescha. Nachor married Melcha, and Abram Sarai. For marriages between uncles and nieces had not yet been forbidden, which in the beginning were celebrated even between brothers and sisters.

« Thare therefore took Abram, » etc., *as far as,* the days of Thare were two hundred and five years, and he died in Haran. » [n: (AUG.)] It is said first, « that [0116A] when Thare was seventy years old he begot Abram, and afterwards remained with all his own in Charan, and died. » And it was said to Abraham that he should go out of Charan, and he went out when he was seventy-five years old. But by recapitulation it is shown that the Lord had given the command while his father was living, etc., *as far as* from whom the Jews were named, on account of the royal tribe.

## CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 1.-- « Go out of the land, » etc. [n: (AUG. ubi supra.)] The narrative of Stephen on this matter is to be considered, etc., *as far as* thus therefore Abram was settled in that land, when he lived there until the birth of Jacob.

[n: (STRAB.)] It is to be noted that he had already gone out of his own land, but had then gone out in body, not [0116B] in mind and will; for he had perhaps a mind of returning, when it was said to him: « Go out, » etc.

[n: (ISID.)] Christ left the land and the kindred of the Jews, and was magnified among the peoples of the Gentiles. We too must go out of our land, that is, out of earthly means and riches, and out of our kindred, that is, the converse of the vices, which, cleaving to us from our birth, are joined to us as it were by the affinities of blood; and « out of the house of our father, » that is, out of the memory of the world, that renouncing it we may be enlarged with the people of God, and, when the time shall come, be brought into the land of the heavenly promise.

VERS. 2-3.--And I will make thee into a great nation, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] Two things are promised to Abraham, namely, that his seed shall possess the land of Chanaan, when it is said: [0116C] « And come into the land which I shall show thee. » But the other far more excellent, not of carnal but of spiritual seed, whereof he is to be the father, not only of the Jewish nation, but of all the Gentiles that follow his faith; which is thus promised: « And in thee shall all the nations of the earth be blessed. »[var: Vulg. Gn 12:3 reads *atque in te benedicentur universae cognationes terrae*, "and in thee shall all the kindreds of the earth be blessed."]

VERS. 4.-- « Seventy-five years old. » [n: (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.)] If Thare, the father of Abraham, in the Chaldean region, etc., *as far as* and tarried there a longer while.

VERS. 8-12.-- « Bethel. » [n: (RAB.)] It is interpreted *the house of God,* which afterwards was called Bethaven, that is, *the house of an idol.*

« Hai. » *Mystically.* Hai is interpreted *eye* or *fountain*. The patriarch therefore, making for the land of promise, pitched his tent between Bethel [0116D] and Hai, because the saints, regenerated by the font of baptism, circumspect in the course of this life, hasten to the tabernacle of God, that is, to the heavenly city.

VERS. 13-14.-- « Say therefore, I beseech thee, » etc. [n: (AUG. lib. XVI de Civ., c. 19.)] It is asked why the patriarch was willing to lie, etc., *as far as* both his own life and his wife's chastity.

VERS. 15-20.-- « The woman was taken up. » [n: (RAB.)] Sarai is excused, who is carried into the house of Pharao unwilling, not only in this, that neither body nor soul is defiled save by the consent of the mind, but also in this, that it was done with her husband agreeing and permitting.

[n: (AUG. Quaest. in Gen.)] With Pharao she could [0117A] be so long untouched, because in Esther it is read that the maidens were not brought in to the kings at once, but were kept for one year, and for six months were anointed with oil of myrrh, and for another six months were among divers perfumes. She could therefore through these delays of time remain untouched. And meanwhile the Egyptians used Abram well, until Pharao, scourged by God and knowing that she was his wife, gave her back untouched.



## CHAPTER XIII

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Abram therefore went up, » etc. [n: (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.)] Delivered out of Egypt, he is rightly read to have gone up, but how could he be exceeding rich? But in the Hebrew verity there stands *exceeding heavy:* for he was weighed down by the burden of Egypt. And [0117B] although riches of gold and of silver may seem to be riches, yet if they are Egyptian they are heavy to a holy man; nor, as it is read in the Seventy, « He went away whence he had come into the desert as far as Bethel, » but, according to the Hebrew, « He went away on his journey by the south as far as Bethel. » For he set out from Egypt, not that he might seek the desert, which he had left behind in Egypt, but that by the south, which is contrary to the north, he might come to the house of God, where his tabernacle was between Bethel and Hai.

VERS. 3-5.-- « In Bethel, » etc. Now Bethel is a little city at the twelfth milestone from Helia, on the right hand as men go to Neapolis: which formerly was called Luza, that is, Almond, in the tribe of Benjamin: but Hai is in the tribe of Juda, set apart for the priests; Bethel, *the house of God;* Hai, *eye* or *fountain:* rightly therefore, pitching his tabernacle [0117C] between Bethel and Hai, he built an altar to the Lord, because the saints, withdrawing from the prince of the world and from the pleasures of the age, washed in the holy font of baptism, leading the course of the present life considerately, and offering to God upon the altar of faith the sacrifice of praise and of good work, tend happily to the house of God, that is, to the heavenly Jerusalem.

VERS. 6-9.-- « For their substance was much, » etc. « Let there not be, I pray thee, » etc. Let us also set nothing before concord and charity, but through the right hand of prosperity, or the left hand of adversity, let us persevere in peace and in love. The quarrel between the shepherds of Lot and the shepherds of Abraham signifies the controversy between catholic pastors and heretical subverters. Whence Lot also signifies the heresiarchs, who is interpreted *declining;* [0117D] and about the Jordan, that is, *a going down,* he chose a region for himself. His shepherds, the heretics and schismatics, who seek out fair and sloping places, and go not up into the mountain with Moses to the Lord, holding peace and unity; and they receive the choice of going whither they will; and fleeing grace, they commit themselves to free will. But the shepherds of Abraham signify catholic teachers, who flee from heretics even in place, according to that to Titus III: « A man that is a heretic, after the first and the second correction, avoid: »[var: Vulg. Tit 3:10 reads *post unam et secundam correptionem*, "after one and a second rebuke."] and this among the nations of the Chananite and the Pherezite, that is, in the land of promise, because between catholics and heretics there is wont to be contention whose is the kingdom of heaven.

[0118A] « For we are brethren. » In three ways are men called brethren in holy Scripture: by nature, as Jacob and Esau; by kindred, as Abram and Lot; by nation, as all the Jews. Whence, if thy brother be impoverished and sell himself to thee, etc.

VERS. 10-12.-- « As a paradise, » etc. Hence we learn that there is an earthly paradise, to which the earthly watering is compared. For this region was flat and of open country, and watered by the flowing Jordan, as Egypt by the Nile.

VERS. 13.-- « Before the Lord. » To whom the punishing of the crime is committed: for the crime was manifest to men also, but not to be punished.

VERS. 14.-- « Lift up thine eyes, » etc. As if to say, look through the four regions of the world; the possession of Palestine [0118B] is promised to him and to his seed. That in certain codices *mare* is put for *occidente*, it comes about because Palestine has the sea on the west.

VERS. 15-17.-- « All the land, » etc. Not this only was promised. For it was not said, I will give thee as much of the land as thou seest; but, I will give thee the land which thou seest. For since land further off was being given on every side, this especially was given which was seen. Whence he added also: « Arise and walk through the land, » etc., namely, that by walking through it he might come to that which, standing in one place, he could not see. But that land is signified which Israel, the seed of Abraham according to the flesh, not according to faith, had received: which was to be as the sand of the sea, because it cannot be numbered.

[0118C] « I will give to thee. » To Christ is promised the inheritance of all nations, who spread his Church through the whole world, to whom the Father says: « Ask of me, and I will give thee the nations for thine inheritance [n: (Psal. II)]. » And in Psalm LXXI: « And he shall rule from sea to sea. »

VERS. 18.-- « In Hebron. » It is a city in the tribe of Juda, which is also called Cariatharbe, that is, *the city of four;* for CHARIAT in Hebrew is [ed: two Hebrew words stand here on Migne's plate, one before CHARIAT and one before *arbe*; our digitized source has lost both, which has run his two italic phrases together into one.] *a city, arbe four.* For there is laid Adam the greatest, and Abram, Isaac and Jacob.



## CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 1.-- « Amraphel. » He is interpreted *he said that it should fall,* and he is king of Sennaar, which is interpreted *the shaking out of teeth* [0118D] or *their stench.* He signifies the heretics, who tend toward a fall, and with deceitful words vomit out a stinking meaning.

« Arioch. » He is interpreted *Drunken* or *drunkenness*: he signifies the folly of the Gentiles, who, drunk with iniquity, worship the creature for the Creator, and are rolled about in the enticements of the world. « Chodorlahomor. » He is interpreted *A comely sheaf*, who is king of Elam, that is, *of this world:* he signifies hypocrisy, which, although it be vain and pernicious, yet seems comely on the surface, but in the time to come what it was shall appear.

« Thadal. » *Knowing the yoke* or *a scout,* who is king of the nations; he signifies avarice, which, shrewd and crafty, subjugates many to itself.

[0119A] « Amraphel, » etc. These four kings figure the four principal vices, contrary to the four principal virtues; which strive to subjugate the five senses signified by the five kings, through the concupiscence of the flesh, through the concupiscence of the eyes, through the ambition of the world.

VERS. 2-13.-- « Balae. » [n: (HIERON.)] In Hebrew it is called *devouring*. The Hebrews hand down that this is elsewhere called *Salissam*, and *a heifer that dismays,* because it was swallowed up by a third earthquake. And that from that time in which Sodom and Gomorrha, Adama and Seboim were overthrown by divine fire, it is named *the little one*. For Segor is translated as little, which in the Syriac tongue is called *Zoara*. But the valley of the Salt-pits, in which were wells of bitumen, after the wrath of God and the rain of brimstone, was turned into the Dead [0119B] Sea. which by the Greeks is called the lake of Asphalt, that is, of bitumen.

*Mystically.* Four kings overcome five, while the contrary powers, abusing the five senses of the body, ensnare them with vices and lead them captive.

*Allegorically.* Such kings overcome first Bara, who is interpreted *malice* or *a creature,* and is king of the Sodomites, that is, *of blindness,* or *of a silent beast,* when they persuade those who persevere in the old malice to follow the blindness of lust after the manner of beasts. They conquer also Bersa, that is, *set in straitness,* king of Gomorrha, that is, of a swelling and seditious people, when they overwhelm and cast down those who are straitened by the cares of the world and fear not to work the wickedness of rapine. They conquer likewise Sennaab king of Adama, that [0119C] is, *of the earthly,* when they exhort those who savour earthly things only to remain in luxury, so that they say: « Let us eat and drink, for tomorrow we shall die [n: (I Cor. XV)]. » They conquer Semeber, who is interpreted *there destruction,* and is king of Seboim, that is, *of the girdle of grief,* when through anger and envy they deceive the incautious; whence Job V: « Anger killeth the foolish, and envy slayeth the little one. » They conquer also king Bale, when they cast the faint-hearted into despair; for Bale is interpreted *a headlong fall* or *a swallowing up:* to this above all the devil strives, that after the fall of sin he may cast into despair.

VERS. 4-6.-- « Twelve, » etc. The number twelve is a superfluous number. In which superfluity our five senses serve the vain superstition which [0119D] Chodorlahomor signifies, and the four vices lying next to it; and when they would withdraw, they suffer a grievous persecution, and are often overcome.

VERS. 7-12.-- « And they smote. » [n: (HIERON.)] Another edition has, etc., *as far as* because there God judged the people.

« Raphaim in Astaroch, »[var: Vulg. Gn 14:5 reads *Raphaim in Astarothcarnaim*.] etc. The names of the places and of the nations signify those who live carnally, whom vices easily overcome.

VERS. 13.-- « Hebrew. » The Seventy, *one from beyond the river;* because, namely, coming from Mesopotamia, the river Euphrates being crossed, he set his seat in the land of Chanaan. Whence Josue the son of Nave: « Why will you serve the gods of your fathers who are beyond the river? »[var: Vulg. Jos 24:14–15 has no such sentence; it reads *auferte deos quibus servierunt patres vestri in Mesopotamia* and *utrum diis, quibus servierunt patres vestri in Mesopotamia*.]

[0120A]VERS. 14-17.-- « And when Abram had heard this, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] *Mystically.* In like manner our faith, when it has been strengthened by the principal Spirit, subjects the five senses of the body as five kings, triumphing by the word of God over the outward man. He overcame his adversaries in three hundred and eighteen, because we, in the sacrament of the cross, which the letter *tau* signifies, which signifies three hundred, overcome the five carnal senses that before held us captive in divers vices. Eighteen, multiplied out of three and six, signifies the faith of the Trinity and the perfection of work, by which the full victory of the virtues against the vices is accomplished.

[n: (STRAB.)] *Allegorically.* The war of Abraham with the four kings signifies the war of the virtues and of the vices. The father therefore of faith, and the first way of believing, fought for his brother [0120B] Lot and conquered, not in a multitude of an army, but in three hundred and eighteen trained servants of his household. So let each man wage war for his own soul against spiritual wickednesses with three hundred and eighteen servants of his household, that is, with the help of the holy cross: for the letter *tau*, which has the figure of a cross, expresses three hundred.

Now Dan is one of the springs of the Jordan; for the other is called Jor, which is interpreted *a stream;* the two springs therefore, which are not far distant from one another, being joined into one streamlet, it is called Jordan.

VERS. 18, 19.-- « But Melchisedech indeed, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] Now the Hebrews say that he is Sem the son of Noah, and by the reckoning of the years they show that he lived until Isaac: and that all the firstborn from Noah to [0120C] Aaron were high priests. But Salem was afterwards called Jerusalem; the Apostle, recording Melchisedech « without father and mother, » refers it to Christ, and through him to the Church of the Gentiles, because the glory of the head is referred to the members. Since he blessed Abraham circumcised, and in him Levi, and so Aaron, from whom afterwards the priesthood. From which he would gather that the priesthood of the Church, which has the uncircumcision, blessed the circumcised priesthood of the Synagogue. But because he says: « Thou art a priest for ever according to the order of Melchisedech, » our ministry of the true order is signified, to be consecrated not through Aaron by the immolating of irrational victims, but by the offering of bread and wine, that is, of the body and blood of the Lord.

[n: (STRAB.)] Melchisedech, who is interpreted *king of justice,* [0120D] and is called *king of peace,* signifies Christ, through whom we have been reconciled, and who at the end of the world is to judge justly.

VERS. 20-23.-- « He gave him tithes. » [n: (ISID.)] The patriarch therefore, knowing, etc., *as far as* partly not denying the stipends due to those who fight lawfully.

VERS. 24.-- « Who came, » etc. For faith sometimes uses the testimonies of unbelievers; whence the Apostle to Titus I: « Evil beasts, slothful bellies; » but to the profit of the faithful.



## CHAPTER XV.

VERS. 1.-- « These things therefore being done, » etc., « Fear not. » When the multitude of vices has been triumphed over, the hope [0121A] of every faithful man is strengthened by the Lord, that it may not waver, but may look for its reward.

VERS. 2.-- « And Abram said. [n: (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.)] « I go without children, » [var: Vulg. Gn 15:2 *ego vadam absque liberis*, "I shall go without children"] etc. *as far as* from him, they say, Damascus was both named and founded.

VERS. 5-8.-- « Look up to heaven, » etc. Above he said: « Thy seed shall be as the sand of the sea, » because in that people some were carnal and earthly, who are compared to sand, since they are dry and unfruitful of good works; others were as shining stars.

[n: (RAB.)] A twofold signification of the promised seed is given. When the stars are shown, it is said to him: « So will I make thy seed, » that is, the Christian nation, whose father thou art to be in faith, namely to glitter [0121B] with the light of the resurrection. But when the sand is shown, it is said: « So shall thy seed be, » etc., that is, the nation of the Jews shall be numerous, but dry and unfruitful.

VERS. 9-10.-- « Take me, said he, » etc. [n: (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.)] The heifer of three years, the Jewish people set under the yoke of the law, etc., *as far as* when by fire the good shall be separated from the evil.

« A heifer of three years, and a she-goat of three years, » etc. Because from Adam to Noah, and thence to Abram, and then to David, the people grew up.

VERS. 11.-- « And the birds came down, » etc. *[n: (GREG., lib. XVI Moral., c. 20.)] Tropologically.* In the very sacrifice of prayer importunate thoughts thrust themselves in, desiring to snatch it away or to violate it: but with the hand of holy discretion we ought to guard what we offer without, [0121C] lest malignant spirits and perverse thoughts snatch it away.

VERS. 12.-- « Dread rushed in, » [var: Vulg. Gn 15:12 *sopor*, "a deep sleep"] etc. [n: (AUG. in Quaest. Hebr.)] This question is to be handled on account of those who contend that these disturbances do not fall upon the mind of the wise man, etc., *as far as* a disturbance not to be named.

VERS. 13-15.-- « Know thou beforehand, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] The people of Israel is prophesied as about to serve in Egypt: not that it served the Egyptians four hundred years, but because this number was completed in that affliction, since it is reckoned from the time at which these things are promised to Abraham.

« Know thou beforehand, » etc. [n: (AUG. de Civ. lib. XVI, c. 24.)] It is not to be so understood, as though the people of God [0121D] had been four hundred years in that most harsh servitude, etc., *as far as* the people of God was afflicted in Egypt with hard servitude.

VERS. 16-21.-- « But in the generation, » etc. [n: (HIERON. epist. ad Damas.)] But Moses says elsewhere, etc., *as far as* and they arrive at Solomon.

## CHAPTER XVI.

VERS. 1.-- « Now Sarai the wife of Abram, » etc. [n: (RAB.)] Abram is no adulterer if, while his wife lives, he is joined to the handmaid, because the Gospel's law of one wife had not yet been promulgated: and he had heard that his stock was to be multiplied, but by what woman he knew not, since he knew Sara to be barren. There was in the intercourse no lustful pleasure, but a devout love of having offspring: [0122A] his wife too willed this and compelled it, she who could not have sons; and therefore she desired to receive from another one whom she might adopt in the place of her own.

VERS. 2-11.-- « Go in to the handmaid, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Note diligently that the procreation of sons is in the Hebrew called *a building up*, etc., *as far as* that, brought back as it were by an angel, she may return into favour and subject herself.

[n: (RAB.)] These two women are the two Testaments: Agar, the Old, which in the Synagogue nourished the Jewish people, liable to servitude; Sara, the New, which begot the Christian people into the liberty of faith. The sons of the handmaid were multiplied, so that they could not be numbered for their multitude, who above are compared to sand. That people therefore, a rival by its own fierceness, and contrary to all good things, and most of all when, with envy goading it, it persecutes the Christian people [0122B] throughout the whole world, and scattered far and wide, is burdensome to all by wandering and by trafficking.

VERS. 12.-- « He shall be a wild man. » Or *a rustic,* according to others. [n: (HIERON.)] In the Hebrew there stands *phara* which is interpreted *a wild ass.* It signifies that his seed shall dwell in the desert, that is, the Saracens, wandering and of uncertain abodes, who, raiding all the nations to whom the desert is joined on the flank, are assailed by all.

VERS. 13.-- « And Agar called. » Rightly did she so call his name, because he freely takes counsel for the wretched and comforts the lowly. Whence: « Thou considerest labour and sorrow, and to thee is the poor man left, » etc. « Verily here have I seen the hinder parts of him that seeth me. » Of like sort is [0122C] that saying: « Thou shalt see my hinder parts, for my face shall not appear to thee. » [var: Vulg. Ex 33:23 *faciem autem meam videre non poteris*, "but my face thou canst not see"] « For man shall not see my face and live [n: (Exod. XXXIII)]. » [var: Vulg. Ex 33:20 *non enim videbit me homo et vivet*, "for man shall not see me and live"] For that manifestation of the wisdom of God no man can see and live. It is that very form for which he sighs who loves God with his whole heart, his whole soul, his whole mind: for the contemplating of which, by loving his neighbour also as himself, so far as he can, he builds up. But his hinder parts, that is, his incarnation, which he took upon him in the last times, we now contemplate by longing, through faith and love, and in the time to come we shall enjoy them in eternal contemplation.

VERS. 14-16.-- « Therefore she called that well, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] To this day the well of Agar is shown in the desert of Arabia, between Cades and Barad, [0122D] which signifies the Scripture of the old law and of the new, which our Creator, the life of the living, the hope of the dying, gave to mortals out of regard of his loving compassion, that being taught and corrected from their vices they may deserve to be for ever worthy of the vision of God.



## CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 1-4.-- . . . . . . « And I will set my covenant, » etc. In the Hebrew *berith* ( ), which signifies not a testament, but a covenant, or a compact.

VERS. 5-7.-- « Neither shall thy name any more be called Abram. » [n: (HIERON.)] The Hebrews say that God added out of his own name, which among them is the tetragrammaton, the letter *h* to Abraham and to Sara. Now he was called [0123A] Abram, which is *an exalted father;* afterward he was called Abraham, which is *a father of many,* for *of nations* is not had in the name, but is understood. Nor is it to be wondered at that, whereas among the Greeks and among us the letter *a* seems to have been added, the letter *h* is said to have been added: for it is the idiom of the Hebrew tongue to write *h* and to read *a*: as, on the contrary, to pronounce *h* and to write *a*.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « And I will give to thee and to thy seed. . . .and all the land of Chanaan. » Chanaan is interpreted *their motion:* whose land is received into possession by the faithful, when the mind, first driven about by divers motions, being changed for the better, is afterward stretched out toward the things before with eyes that turn not back

« Into an everlasting possession. » [n: (AUG.)] Scripture calls that eternal whose end is not appointed, or that which [0123B] is not so done that it is not thenceforth to be done, so far as pertains to the care or the power of the doer; whence: He shall serve for ever who shall not know how to use a little.


VERS. 10.-- « The male among you shall be circumcised, » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] For three causes is this enjoined upon Abraham. First, that he might please by the obedience of the commandment, by the transgression whereof Adam had displeased; secondly, that by this sign he might be distinguished from the other nations; thirdly, that being circumcised in that member wherein lust is wont to have dominion, he might know that chastity was to be kept by him and unchastity to be cut away.

VERS. 11-13.-- « The flesh of the foreskin. » [n: (RAB. STRAR.)] Circumcision made in one member signifies the spiritual circumcision, which is to be shown forth in the heart and in all [0123C] the senses. The heart is to be circumcised, that we think not hurtful things; the eyes, that we see not vanity; the ears are to be circumcised, that is, to be stopped, that they hear not blood: and so in all the senses circumcision is to be celebrated.

[n: (ALCUIN. in Gen., tom. I.)] And circumcision was for this cause commended to Abraham, etc., *as far as* whence of old transgressors begot unto death.

*Allegorically.* Even then, because of the pilgrimage to come, lest his seed should be mingled with the nations, the compact of circumcision is given for a sign: because it signifies nature renewed through baptism, after the stripping off of the old man through Christ, who rose again after baptism. But he commanded the home-born servants, and the bought ones also, to be circumcised, that he might teach that the grace of redemption pertains to all [0123D]. There too the names of the parents are changed, that all things may sound of newness. Abram, *an exalted father;* Abraham, *a father of many nations.*

VERS. 14.-- « The male whose flesh of his foreskin, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] It is asked: how ought circumcised [cj: *circumcisi*; read *incircumcisi*, "uncircumcised"] boys (who did not make void the compact of God, but they who neglected to circumcise them) to perish? But they made void the compact of God not according to their deed, but according to their origin in the first man, in whom all have sinned. For all are born sinners, not properly but originally. Therefore all perish who are not set free through regeneration.

VERS. 15, 16.-- « Sarai thy wife, » etc. [0124A] [n: (ISID.)] The promise concerning the calling of the Gentiles is declared in Isaac, the son of promise, that is, of grace, not of nature, since he is of an aged father and a barren mother: and because this was to be not by generation, as Ismael was, but by regeneration, circumcision was commanded, when the son of Sara is promised in the type of the Church, not when Ismael is, who is the type of the Jews.

[n: (HIERON.-ISID.)] They err who think that Sara was at first written with one *r*, and that afterward another *r.* was added. And because *r* among the Greeks signifies *a hundred*, they surmise many follies about this name, whereas, however they may wish the word to have been changed, it ought to have not a Greek but a Hebrew reckoning. For it is Hebrew. For no one, calling anyone whatsoever in one language, takes the etymology of the word from [0124B] another. Sarai therefore was at first called ( ) by *scin, res, iod.* When therefore the *iod* was taken away, the letter *he* was added, which is read for *a*, and she was called *Sara* ( ). For before she was called *my princess,* because she was as it were the mother of one family: afterward, absolutely, *a princess,* being namely about to be princess of all nations. Some most wretchedly surmise that she was first called *leprosy,* and afterward *a princess,* since leprosy is called *sarai* ( ), which in our tongue seems to have some likeness, but in the Hebrew is utterly different. For it is written by *zadich* and *ain* and *res* and *tau;* which manifestly differs from the three above, that is, *schin, res* and *he,* by which *Sara* is written.

VERS. 17, 18.-- « Abraham fell upon his face, and laughed in his heart. » [n: (HIERON.)] A little after [0124C] there follows: « And thou shalt call his name Isaac: » which laughter is interpreted of the laughter of Abraham, not of the laughter of Sara. For after his son was called Isaac from the laughter of Abraham, Sara is read to have laughed.

« His name Isaac. » It is to be known that four in the Old Testament were called openly by their own names before they were born: Ismael, Isaac, Samson and Josias. Read the Scriptures.

VERS. 19.-- « I will establish my compact, » etc. But the compact of the Lord is with Isaac, that is, with the faithful people, whom mother Church has begotten through faith and baptism. « And an everlasting covenant, » because the possession of eternal life is promised to him alone who with his head, Christ, shall possess the kingdom of heaven for ever.

VERS. 20.-- « Upon Ismael, » etc. *Historically.* [0124D] Ismael signifies the people of the Jews, which was multiplied exceedingly; he begot twelve princes, since the multitude of carnal Jews, begotten of the twelve patriarchs, was divided into twelve tribes.

VERS. 21.-- « In the other year. » The other year, wherein Sara bore Isaac, is the times of regeneration, in which the blood of Christ was poured out and the human race restored to life.

VERS. 22.-- « God went up to Abraham, » [var: Vulg. Gn 17:22 *ascendit Deus ab Abraham*, "God went up from Abraham"] etc. [n: ( Glossa Graeca. )] Therefore, so to speak, he had come down, and had shown himself humble, in appointing the law of circumcision.

VERS. 23-27.-- « And Abraham took, » etc. God did not intend that only the foreskin of the flesh of Abraham [0125A] and of his offspring should be circumcised, but upon him whom he had chosen out of all he was conferring the gifts of the eternal Testament; whence Paul, Phil. III: « For we are the circumcision, who serve God in the spirit. » And elsewhere, Rom. II: « For it is not he that is in the open who is a Jew, neither is that circumcision which is in the open, in the flesh. » Let us therefore circumcise by the word of God our ears, our lips, our heart, our foreskin and all the members of the flesh. Of the circumcision of the ears it is said, Luc. VIII: « He that hath ears to hear, let him hear; » with uncircumcised ears we cannot hear the words of wisdom and of truth. Let us circumcise our lips, that we speak nothing base, or evil, or idle. He circumcises the flesh of the foreskin who uses marriage for the sole cause of posterity, at fixed and lawful seasons: he who does otherwise [0125B] does not circumcise. But the Church, strengthened by the grace of Christ crucified for her, refrains herself not only from things unlawful, but even from things allowed, and as a virgin spouse of Christ flourishes with chaste virgins, in whom the true circumcision of the foreskin is kept in the flesh. But he who does not commit adultery by obscene desires, or by heretical opinions and blasphemous assertions, but keeps the purity of faith in his conscience, is circumcised in heart: and of him it can be said: « Blessed are the clean of heart [n: (Matth. V)], » etc. Let the hands be circumcised, that they do no evil, the feet, that they run not into evil: the sight, that it covet not unlawful things; the taste, that it eat and drink to the glory of God; the smell, that it may take the odour of Christ, and by works of mercy scatter abroad the odour of sweetness. Each several member also, if it serve God in its offices, [0125C] is circumcised. If they run riot beyond the law prescribed, uncircumcised.

[n: (HIERON.-STRAB.)] It is wont to be asked: how were women saved, who were not circumcised? By faith, namely, and by the offering of victims, as before circumcision holy men were cleansed whence: « Purifying their hearts by faith. »



## CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- « And the Lord appeared to him in the vale of Mambre. » [n: (ISID. in Gen. )] Abraham bears a threefold figure: the first, of the Saviour, who leaving his kindred came into the world; the second, of the Father, because he offered up his only Son; the third (which appears in this place), of the saints who received the coming of Christ with joy [0125D]. The tabernacle of Abraham signifies Jerusalem, where in their season the prophets and the apostles dwelt, and Christ at his coming was received by them that believed, and by them that believed not was hanged upon the tree.

VERS. 2, 3.-- « Three men. » Three men announce the coming of Christ, whom two angels accompany, Moses according to some, and Elias; the one a lawgiver, who in the law showed the coming of the Lord: the other, who is to come at the end, to announce the second coming of Christ and to preach his Gospel. Whence, when the Lord was transfigured on the mountain, they are seen with him. « He saw three, and adored one, » showing namely the Saviour, whose coming he was awaiting. Whence: « Abraham saw my day and was [0126A] glad [n: (Joan. VIII)]. » [var: Vulg. Jo 8:56 *Abraham pater vester exsultavit ut videret diem meum : vidit, et gavisus est*, "Abraham your father rejoiced that he might see my day: he saw it, and was glad"] For he foresaw the mystery of the sacrament to come. Whence he washed their feet, that he might show the purification of the laver that was to be at the end of the world; for the feet signify the last things.

« Whom when he had seen. » *[n: (GREG., lib. IX Moral., c. 51.)] Morally.* Abraham ran to meet three angels, because the man and master of the house, that is, the spiritual understanding, ought in the knowledge of the Trinity to pass beyond the barriers of the flesh, and as it were to go out at the door of the lowest dwelling. Let the care of the flesh, which Sara signifies, not appear abroad for ostentation, but behind the man's back, that is, under the discretion of the spirit, intent upon necessary things alone, let it not know how to uncover itself shamelessly, but to govern itself modestly.

[n: (Ibid., lib. XXVIII, c. 4)]. Abraham could not have seen the angels, received them into his lodging and set food before them, [0126B] unless they had taken a body out of air: for they would not appear, they would not take food, unless they had a solid body of a heavenly element. Nor is it strange that now they are called angels, now the Lord, because they are called angels who minister without, and the Lord is shown, who commands them within.

VERS. 4, 5.-- « But I will fetch. » He returns to the offices of hospitality, which can be shown even to angels, when they take a bodily form out of the elements: which Abraham understanding, adored the Godhead in unity, and ministered to the angelic dispensation in visible form.

VERS. 6.-- « Three sata. » These things are said in the type of the Church, which out of the three parts of the world is gathered into the unity of faith, and as bread without leaven [0126C] is baked by the fire of the Holy Ghost.

[n: (STRAB.)] A satum is a kind of measure. Three sata signify the three sons of Noah, out of whom the whole race of men was sown abroad. Therefore the Church is signified, or the evangelical doctrine that is to be sown throughout the whole world, and to be multiplied in the three parts of the world, Asia, Africa and Europe.

« Three sata, » of which Sara makes the loaves baked under the ashes, signify the three sons of Noah, out of whom the whole race of men was born: who, believing in the divine Trinity, were sprinkled with the water of baptism through Sara, that is, the Church, and were brought together into the one bread of the body of Christ. These are the three sata of meal which the woman in the Gospel leavened: they are unleavened loaves, because without the leaven of malice, and the anguish [ *Al.,* rancore] [0126D] of wickedness, and the fervour of perverse doctrine, the unity of believers ought to be.

« Baked under the ashes. » They are baked under the ashes, that being baked by the vapour of the Holy Ghost through repentance for their sins, they may be made food acceptable to God.

VERS. 7.-- « A calf most tender. » The tender and fatted calf is the body of Christ, which for the salvation of the world was offered up upon the tree of the cross. This is the calf that is killed for the prodigal son.

VERS. 8.-- « Butter and milk » he set before them with the flesh of the calf, because milk signifies the old law; whence I Cor. III: « Milk I gave you to drink, not meat, » etc. For he had delivered to them the commandment of the law, as milk pressed out of the breasts of the two tables, that is [0127A] the testament of faith, because they could not yet take the solid meat of the evangelical doctrine. The most rich and fat butter is the evangelical testimony, which like oil is given to the faithful for a sign. He set the calf before them with milk and butter, because neither can the body of the Lord be without the milk of the law, nor this without the Gospel. And they sat under the tree in sign of the Lord's passion, whereof they are the preachers.

VERS. 9.-- « Behold she is in the tabernacle, » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] Abraham is without with the angels, Sara is in the tabernacle: because it belongs to the strong and the learned to preach openly, to instruct by examples: to the weak and the unwise, to learn and to be subject.

« VERS. 10.--And she shall have a son, » etc. God promises a son to the barren Sara, saying: About this time [0127B] I will come; he signifies not the time, but the quality of his coming, since through the son promised again the faithful people was to be born. Whence Isa. LIV and Gal. IV: « Rejoice, thou barren, that travailest not, » etc.

« Which when she had heard, etc. [n: (AUG. Quaest in Gen. )] Sara is reproved because she laughed, and not Abraham, although he had laughed in like manner; because Abraham's laughter was of wonder, Sara's of doubt, which he could discern who knows the heart. Sara denies that she laughed, whereas she cannot be hidden from God, but perhaps she thought that they were men. But Abraham understood God, yet in rendering the offices of humanity, which are necessary only to weak flesh; it is strange: unless he first judged them men, but men in whom he understood God to speak, certain signs of the divine majesty appearing: as Scripture often testifies that God [0127C] appeared in men, but afterward they knew that they had been angels.

VERS. 11.-- « And they were, » etc. The age of the elderly is less than that of the old, even though the old be called elderly. Whence, according to the physicians, an elderly man cannot get children of an elderly woman, even though the courses of women still flow. According to this Abraham wonders at the power of God concerning the promise of a son, and we can take it that the Apostle set down this miracle. For the body was not altogether dead for generation, if the woman had been of youthful age: but of one of advanced age he could not beget: for of Cethura he afterward begot, because she was young. For the physicians hand down that an old man cannot beget of a woman of more advanced age (even though the courses of women befall her), [0127D] but of a young one he can. Again a woman of advanced age, even though the courses of women still befall her, cannot bear by an elderly man, but by a young one she can. It was therefore a miracle, because both were old and of advanced age, and the courses of women had ceased for Sara.

VERS. 12-14.-- « Shall I give myself to pleasure? » etc. [n: (GREG. lib. IX Moral., c. 51.)] The care of the flesh, etc., *as far as* what else does our mind bring forth than joy?

VERS. 15.-- « I did not laugh, etc. [n: (AMBR.)] Or she therefore denied that she had laughed, because she was ignorant; she therefore laughed, because she prophesied.

VERS. 17-19.-- « Can I hide from Abraham what « I am about to do. » Well does the Lord commit his secrets to Abraham, [0128A] whom he knew to be a faithful executor of his will and of his secrets; of whom he himself was to be born in the flesh, « in whom are all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge hidden [n: (II Cor. II)]. » He promises also to Abraham not only rewards, but the justice of the obedience of his sons, and that the promises should be fulfilled concerning them. And again Isaac is promised, « about to be a great nation, » and that « in him shall all the nations of the earth be blessed. » [var: Vulg. Gn 18:18 *benedicendae sint in illo omnes nationes terrae*, "all the nations of the earth shall be blessed in him"] By which words two things are promised him: the Jewish nation according to the flesh, and all the Gentiles through faith.

VERS. 20.-- « The cry of Sodom and Gomorrha, » etc. [n: (AUG. Q. in Gen. )] If we take these words as those not of one doubting, but of one angry and threatening, there is no question. For after the manner of men God speaks [0128B] to men, and his anger is without disturbance, because he is unchangeable. But often in threatening we say thus: Let us see whether I do not do it to thee, and whether I shall not be able to do it to thee; or, let me know, that is, let me try whether I cannot. But disturbance does not fall upon God. It is the custom of human speech, to which God fits himself, that he may suit himself to human weakness.

[n: (GREG. lib. V Moral., cap. 7.)] But sin with a voice is a fault in action. But sin with a cry is a fault with liberty.

VERS. 21-23.-- « I will go down and see, » etc. [n: (GREG., Moral., c. 33.)] Let us not believe men's evil deeds before we prove them. And behold, through angels he came down to know their evils, and finding them wrapped in so great a crime [0128C], he as it were passed over his patience, and forestalled them with the fire of judgment before the day of judgment: who judges with tranquillity, that the greater crimes may be slowly believed, and, when truly known, more swiftly punished.

[n: (GREG., lib. I Reg., c. 178, tom. I.)] Whither should God go down, that he might know what things had been done? Or what should he not know, who is everywhere? But that he might give to our ignorance an example of discretion, that we ought not to believe grievous evils on hearing them, he says of himself that he goes down to know, of whom it is plain to all that even without going down he would know all things.

[n: (HILAR., lib. IX, de Trin.)] We have God not knowing that which nevertheless he is not ignorant of. For since he knows the sins to be exceeding great, he goes down again that he may see whether they are consummated; and if they are not yet consummated [0128D], that he may know: we understand that he does not then know them because he was ignorant before; but that he then knows, because it is the time for acting. Therefore for God not to know is not a diminution of ignorance, but the fulness of time.

VERS. 24-26.-- « If there be fifty just men, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] The number fifty, from which he descends down to ten, signifies penance. Whence also the fiftieth psalm is penitential. If therefore they had been converted, they would have been saved. But when God sees the offenders unwilling to be converted to penance, straightway he restrains the heat of immoderate luxury with the fire of hell. He said that up to ten just men Sodom should not perish, because if in any man the name of Christ be found through the keeping of the ten commandments, he shall not [0129A] perish. For the figure of the number ten signifies also the cross of Christ.

VERS. 27.-- « I will speak to the Lord, » etc. *[n: (GREG. lib. XVIII Moral., c. 33.)] Mystically.* The more the saints look into the inward things of the Godhead, the more do they see themselves to be nothing. Nowhere is Abraham read to have professed himself ashes and dust, save when he deserved to have speech with God. And he said: « I will speak to my Lord, whereas I am dust, » etc. For perhaps he would have believed himself to be something, if he had not felt the true essence which is above him. Whence David also cries out: « Remember, Lord, that I am dust [n: (Psal. X)], » etc.



## CHAPTER XVIII. (cont.)

*Morally.* Some it lifts up not through the swelling of understanding, but through works of mercy; certain men, while they see themselves [0129B] to abound in earthly things, do not seek the true riches, and love not the eternal kingdom, because they think temporal things sufficient. The fault therefore is not in the understanding, but in the affection. For all things which God founded are good. But he who uses good things ill, acts so that through the gluttony of voracity he dies by that bread whereby he ought to have lived. Lazarus the poor man had come to rest, torments were racking the proud rich man [n: (Luc. XVI)]: yet Abraham had been rich, who held Lazarus in his bosom. Yet he says: « I will speak to my Lord, whereas I am dust and ashes, » etc. How did he value his riches, who accounted himself dust and ashes? Openly we discern in what place he had set himself, who accounted himself dust and ashes even while he spoke with God. With what punishment therefore are they to be struck, who [0129C] make no progress toward the highest things and exalt themselves over the least?

VERS. 28-33.-- « There be five. » Five cities were burned with showers of fire. For all who have handled the five senses of the body lustfully are to be burned in the fire to come.

« What if there be found, » etc. [n: (ALC.)] It is not necessary to take this of every place. But of Sodom it could be said, because God knew that there were not even ten there, and therefore it was thus answered to Abraham, to the aggravating of the iniquity of the Sodomites

« Ten. » The number ten designates the cross of Christ, because the letter X, by which ten is expressed, is formed in the figure of a cross.

« He said. » For he knew that there was not even one there besides [0129D] Lot; this is said to the aggravating of their iniquity, as in Jeremias: « See if you find a man doing justice [n: (Jer. V)]; [var: Vulg. Jr 5:1 *an inveniatis virum facientem judicium*, "whether you can find a man doing judgment"] as if to say: Find but one, and I spare them.



## CHAPTER XIX.

VERS. 1.-- « And there came two angels, » etc. It is to be noted that three angels appear to Abraham in an odd number, because God rejoices in an odd number. To Sodom they came in an even number, that is, two, which number is the first that departs from unity, and is the chief of otherness, figuring as it were a womanish softness. They came in the « evening, » because the sun of justice had already set for the Sodomites. « Who when he had seen, » etc. While Lot runs to meet them [0130A] and adores, he seems to understand that they are angels; while he invites them to the refreshing of the body, he seems to think that they are men. So of these and of those who appeared to Abraham the same is to be understood. And from this place of Scripture and from many others the virtue of hospitality is commended. Whence Paul, Heb. XIII: « Forget not hospitality: for by this some have pleased God, having received angels to their lodging. » [var: Vulg. Hbr 13:2 *per hanc enim latuerunt quidam, angelis hospitio receptis*, "for by this some have entertained angels unawares"]

[n: ( Glossa Graeca. )] It is to be noted that where the benefits of God are set forth, God is present; but where punishments are executed, angels are sent; that we may know that to do good is proper to God, delightful and pleasing to God, but that to take vengeance is unwelcome to God.

VERS. 2.-- « Wash. » Which is wont to be done for guests, that the affections, soiled with earthly dust, and with sloth, and [0130B] with faint-heartedness, as with dusty filth, may be cleansed. Whence in monasteries a prayer is celebrated for those who come from the road.

VERS. 3-7.-- « He pressed them. » [n: (ALC.)] The angels as though compelled enter the house of Lot, that his charity, being tried and approved, might be rewarded, and that it might be shown how great a good hospitality is. They entered a hospitable house, and set the host free; the fire, entering the houses that were shut to guests, destroyed the sinners.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « I have two daughters. » [n: (AUG., Q. in Gen.)] It is deservedly asked: is a balancing of outrages, or of any sins whatever, to be admitted, that we may do evils lest worse be done: or is it to be attributed to Lot's disturbance, and not to his counsel? and doubtless [0130C] this balancing is admitted most perilously. But if it was a disturbance of mind, it is in no wise to be imitated.

VERS. 10.-- « And they brought in Lot to them. » [n: (GREG., l. VI Moral., cap. 16.)] While the wicked oppose him, Lot is brought back within the house and fortified: because every just man, bearing the snares of the depraved, returns to his mind, and remains undismayed. The Sodomites cannot find the door in the house of Lot: because the corrupters of minds find no entrance of reproof against the life of the just man. Struck with blindness they go round the house as it were, because in their envy they scrutinize his deeds and words. But because on every side, out of the just man's life, a strong and praiseworthy action meets them, they wander and grope at the wall.

VERS. 11-13.-- « And they struck them with blindness. » *(AUG. [0130D] ubi supra.)* In Greek ἀορασία, that is, *avidentia,* if it may be so called, which causes that a thing be not seen, not all things, but that which is not needful. For how would they fail in seeking the door, if they had been struck with blindness so that they saw nothing? for troubled by their own calamity they would have sought nothing further. With this ἀορασία were they struck who sought Eliseus, and who, walking with Christ after the resurrection, did not know him.

VERS. 14.-- « He went out, » etc. [n: (HIER., in Q. Hebr.)] In what follows the daughters of Lot are read to have been virgins; and he himself said to the Sodomites: « Behold I have two daughters who have not known man, » [var: Vulg. Gn 19:8 *Habeo duas filias, quae necdum cognoverunt virum*, "I have two daughters who have not yet known man"] etc. But now he is said to have had sons-in-law. But some think that those who had husbands perished in Sodom, and that those who were virgins [0131A] went out with their father. But the Hebrew verity has. « Lot went out, and spoke to the spouses who were to receive his daughters: » therefore his daughters, being virgins, had not yet been joined in marriage.

VERS. 15, 16.-- « And when it was morning, » etc. *[n: (ISID.)] Mystically.* Lot, the brother of Abraham, just and hospitable in Sodom, signifies the body of Christ, which in all the saints groans among the wicked, to whose deeds it does not consent, and from whose commingling it shall be set free at the end of the world, when they are condemned to the punishment of eternal fire

VERS. 17-19.-- « In the mountain. » [n: (STRAB.)] *Morally.* The mountain signifies the height of the virtues, to which the angel exhorts, according to that of Marci XIX. « If thou wilt be perfect, go and sell all that thou hast, » [var: Vulg. Mt 19:21 *vade, vende quae habes, et da pauperibus*, "go, sell what thou hast, and give to the poor"] etc. But he who shall see that he cannot climb up, it is better [0131B] that he remain in Segor, that is, in the little one, content with a lay and common life, than that he seize upon the mountain of virtue and return to secular things. Virginity is a high mountain, to which the angel exhorts: if any man sees that he cannot climb up, let him remain in Segor, that is, in lawful wedlock, because it is better to use a middling good than to be hurled down the precipices of lust. Whence I Cor. VII: « Let every man have a wife because of fornication. » « Neither can I in the mountain: » that is, being disturbed he did not believe the Lord, whom he recognized in the angels, as being disturbed he prostituted his daughters.

VERS. 20.-- « There is a city, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] *Allegorically.* Sodom is interpreted *blindness,* and expresses worldly desires: the height of the mountains, the contemplation of the perfect. But because many flee the enticements of the world, and yet [0131C] being set in action cannot reach the summit of contemplation, Lot went out from Sodom, but did not come to the mountains. Whence Lot says: « There is this city near, » etc. It is called « Near, » and yet it is safe for salvation, because the active life is neither wholly severed from the cares of the world, nor a stranger to the joy of eternal salvation.

« To which I can, » etc. Those who are not able to ascend to the contemplative life flee to the active life, in which they are saved.

*[n: (GREG., Past. III, 28.)] Morally.* To flee burning Sodom, etc., *as far as* whence it is said to Lot:

VERS. 21-23.-- « Behold also in this I have heard thy prayers, » because when prayer is poured out to God, such a life of wedlock is not condemned; whence I Cor. VII: « Do not [0131D] defraud one another, » etc.

VERS. 24.-- « And the Lord rained upon Sodom, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] Brimstone, the stench of the flesh, fire, the burning of carnal desire; when the Lord had determined to punish the crimes of the flesh, by the quality of the vengeance the stain of the crime was made known. For brimstone stinks, fire burns; they therefore who had burned unto perverse desires with the stench of the flesh, perished justly by brimstone and fire, that from a just punishment they might learn what they had done by unjust desire.

[n: (STRAB.)] It is to be noted that the water of the flood destroyed the first sinners, who in like manner had offended God by the concupiscence of the flesh. But the Sodomites perished by a heavier punishment, namely by a flood of fire and brimstone. For although those exceeded the measure, they sinned as it were naturally [0132A]; but these against nature: therefore they were condemned by the example of the heaviest punishment.

VERS. 25.-- « All the inhabitants. » [n: (ALC.)] The infants were burned with their parents in Sodom, lest the most impious deed of the Sodomites could be turned aside, if any trace of their stock remained. And it was provided for them, lest living long they should follow the examples of their fathers and be punished more grievously. The parents also are guilty as much for themselves as for them. For the death of the sons is the crime of the parents. Therefore they shall be their accusers. It is also some kind of benefit, for him who is not glorious, not to be guilty; for it profits him who cannot be a king not to be poor.

VERS. 26-28.-- « And his wife looking back. » [n: (ISID.)] *Mystically.* The wife figures the kind of those who, being called by grace [0132B], look back, and strive to return to the things they had left; of whom it is said, Luc. IX: « No man putting his hand on the plough, and looking back, is fit for the kingdom of God. » Therefore it is forbidden them to look back. Wherein it is shown that there is no returning to the old life for those who have been regenerated through grace, if they desire to escape the last burning; the wife looking back was turned into salt, being made namely an example, and a seasoning whereby others may be salted. Whence Christ says, Luc. XVII: « Remember the wife of Lot, » that he might season us as it were with salt, that we should not be negligent, but should beware prudently.

VERS. 29.-- « For when he overthrew, » etc. Scripture intimates that God delivered Lot rather by the merits of Abraham, that we may understand that he was called just in a certain manner, [0132C] chiefly because he worshipped the one and true God, and by comparison with the Sodomites, among whom living he could not be bent to the like crime. « He remembered Abraham. » It is plain that Lot was delivered by the merits of Abraham, whom Scripture calls just. And it is to be known that he was just, not as Abraham was, but by comparison with the Sodomites.

VERS. 30.-- « And he went up, » etc. So the married sometimes ascend from the lowliness of the active life to the height of the contemplative.

« And Lot went up from Segor, » etc. [n: (HIER. in Q. Hebr., tom. III.)] It is asked why, when at first he preferred Segor to flight into the mountain, etc., *as far as* he ought not to have doubted.

VERS. 31.-- « And the elder said to the younger, » etc. [0132D] Which is said in excuse of the daughters, because they thought that the human race had failed, and therefore lay with their father. But that which follows does not excuse the father, « and he knew not that they had slept with him; » for the Hebrews mark it above with an obelus, as superfluous, as though it were incredible, because the nature of things does not admit that anyone should have intercourse unknowing.

[n: (STRAB.)] Lot is inexcusable, first because he did not believe the angel; then because he was made drunk, and sin was the cause of sin.

[n: (HIERON.)] So depraved suggestion and delight, like incestuous women, lie in wait for the continent, that, having taken occasion, they may steep with the wine of deceit those who are torpid, and cast them down from the state of uprightness as though unwitting.

[0133A]VERS. 32.-- « Come, let us make him drunk, » etc. [n: (ALC.)] The two daughters of Lot had learned that the consummation of the world was to be by fire: but, as girls not perfect, neither did they know that, when the Sodomites had been laid waste by fire, much space remained entire; and they supposed that some such thing had been done as they had heard of in the times of Noah, and that they were left surviving with their father for the repairing of the human race. And although the lying with their father was a great crime, a heavier impiety seemed if, keeping their chastity, they should destroy the hope of posterity. Therefore they take counsel, and soften their father's sadness or sternness with wine, and each on her several night receives conception from him without his knowledge, and they do not repeat it.

VERS. 33-35.-- « They gave, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] Lot in this place signifies the law, because certain men begotten of her, [0133B] and set under her, by understanding ill do in a manner make themselves drunk; and by using her not lawfully, they bring forth the works of unbelief. For the law is good, if a man use it lawfully.

VERS. 36.-- « They conceived therefore, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] Moab ( ) is interpreted *from the father,* and the whole name has its etymology. But Ammon, of whom as it were the reason of the name is rendered, *son of my kindred,* or, as it is better in the Hebrew, *benammi* ( ) that is *son of my people,* is so derived that in part it is the sense of the name, in part the word itself. For Ammon, from whom the Ammonites, is called *my people.*

VERS. 37, 38. « She called his name Moab, » etc. [n: (THEOD.)] This one seems the more shameless, who wished to transmit her incest even to posterity. For Moab [0133C] signifies *from the father*: the other the more modest, who gave her son the name Benammi, that is *son of my people.*



## CHAPTER XX.

VERS. 1.-- « Setting forward thence, » etc. Cades, *the fountain of judgment;* Sur, *narrowness;* by which patience is figured.

VERS. 2-6.-- « And he said of Sara, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is commonly asked how at that age Abraham could fear peril on account of Sara's beauty: but the force of her form is to be wondered at, which could still be loved.

« And he said of Sara, » etc. [n: (ISID., in Gen., tom. VI.)] He was silent that she was his wife, and said she was his sister, lest he himself should be killed and she be possessed as a captive by strangers, being sure that she would be kept inviolate by God, as at Pharaoh's court, [0133D] etc., *as far as* « Call not [any man] father upon earth, for one is your Father who is in heaven. »

« And he said, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] As to what is said to Abimelech on Sara's behalf, « And I spared thee, that thou shouldst not sin against me, » [var: Vulg. *custodivi te*, "I kept thee"] it is to be observed that God did this when he warned him that she whom he thought a sister was Abraham's wife. And it is to be noted that sin is committed against God when such things are done as men think either not to be sins, or to be reckoned light, as being sins in the flesh.

VERS. 7-11.-- « He shall pray for thee, » etc. [n: (CHRYSOST., hom. 45 in Gen.)] But know thou that his prayers shall grant thee life.

VERS. 12.-- « Otherwise also, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Since [0134A] in the Hebrew there stands *vegam* ( ) that is, also truly « she is my sister, the daughter of my father, but not the daughter of my mother, » and it sounds rather that she was Abraham's sister. In excuse we say that such marriages were not yet forbidden by law.

« The daughter of my father. » Another translation, more plainly, « from a father, and not from a mother; » for she was the daughter of Aran, Abraham's brother, and Lot's sister.

VERS. 13.-- « But after God brought me out of my father's house, I said to her, » etc. After the word of God was rejected from Judaea, all the faithful are Christ's brothers and sisters; whence Matth. XII: « Whosoever shall do the will of my Father, who is in heaven, he is my brother, and sister, and mother. »

[0134B]VERS. 14.-- « He took therefore. » Allegorically Abraham signifies Christ, Sara the Church. Abraham is honored on account of Sara's beauty, and Christ on account of the Church; whence psal. LXVII: « God is wonderful in his saints. » Sara had a husband, but he lay hidden; the Church has Christ, who is not seen in the world, being received into heaven.

VERS. 15.-- « Wheresoever it shall please thee, » etc. They who had once put off believing through fear of princes, at the bidding of those same princes betook themselves to the Church, which is Christ's sister and wife, and spread abroad the glory of Christ and of the Church through all the parts of the world.

VERS. 16.-- « A thousand pieces of silver, » etc. He speaks as though in jest: because she called him her brother who [0134C] was her husband, and he exhorts her to remember this hereafter, lest she afterwards, being caught, incur a like reproach. A thousand pieces of silver are given to Christ when eloquent teachers of the world are taken up out of Gentile converse to hold and to preach the faith of Christ. For the number of a thousand, squared from the number ten, designates the stable and solid doctrine of evangelical preaching.

VERS. 17.-- « And when he prayed. » When the Son prays the Father, the barrenness of worldly men, and of princes, and of subject peoples is healed.



## CHAPTER XXI.

VERS. 1-3.-- « And God visited Sara, » [var: Vulg. *Dominus*, "the Lord"] etc. The figure of circumcision is manifold. For it is [0134D] according to the Apostle a seal of the justice of faith, to Abraham and to his seed, and a judgment for chastising those who should belong to this seed and faith; and there arises from this seed a prophecy of the Saviour, who should cleanse us in the present life from all sin through baptism, and in the time to come, after the resurrection, free us from all corruption of death. And the number eight befits the glory of the resurrection: for the Lord rose on the eighth day, that is, after the seventh, the sabbath; and we, after the six ages of this world and the seventh, the sabbath, that is, the rest of souls which meanwhile is passed in the other life, shall rise again as at an eighth season, stripped of all the carnal corruptions and vices of concupiscence (in which lust chiefly reigns).

[0135A]VERS. 4.-- « He circumcised » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] Although circumcision was enjoined for many causes, it holds forth in a special way the figure of Christ, who through baptism strips us of the old man, and in the time to come, after the resurrection, will circumcise away all mortality and corruption. And in that Isaac was circumcised on the eighth day, the eighth age of the world is designated, when the resurrection of the saints shall be generally celebrated.

VERS. 8.-- « The child grew therefore, » etc. He grows who, disposing ascents in his heart, comes to the solid food of doctrine. Whence, grow, and thou shalt eat me.

« He grew, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] Not on the day of his son's birth, nor on the day of his circumcision, but on the day of his weaning did he make a feast: because great ought to be the joy [0135B] of the spiritual age, when a man has been made such as may be fed with solid food, like to those to whom it is said I Cor. III: « I gave you milk to drink, not meat. »

VERS. 9.-- « And when Sara had seen. » etc. [n: (HIER.)] This is expounded in two ways: either that he made sport for idols, according to that of Exod. XXXII: « The people sat down to eat and to drink, and they rose up to play. » Or that against Isaac, as standing in the place of the elder in age, he claimed the birthright for himself; which Sara hearing did not bear.

VERS. 10.-- « Cast out this bondwoman, » etc. [n: (BED., c. 21; ISID., c. 17, in Gen.)] It is asked: why did Sara before wish to receive a son by the bondwoman, or why does she now bid him be cast out [0135C] with his mother? Which she does not do inflamed with jealousy, but compelled by the mystery of prophecy. For Agar, according to the Apostle, brought forth the carnal people unto bondage; but Sara the spiritual and free people, with which freedom Christ has set us free. It was signified therefore by this mystery that the former people, begotten in bondage, does not remain in the house of Sara, that is, of the Church, for ever, nor is heir or partner of Christians, nor possesses the kingdom of glory together with the noble son, that is, the faithful people.

VERS. 11, 12.-- « He took it hard, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is asked why Abraham was made sorrowful, since this was a prophecy which he rather than Sara ought to have known: but Sara said from revelation what had first been revealed to her. He however was moved [0135D] by a father's affection, to whom the Lord afterwards revealed it. Or perhaps both of them at first knew not what it was, and it was prophesied through Sara who knew it not, when she was moved with a woman's mind on account of the bondwoman's pride.

VERS. 13.-- « For thy seed. » [n: (AUG.)] It is to be noted that Ismael also was called by God the seed of Abraham, whereas the Apostle says Rom. IX: « Not the children of the flesh, but the children of the promise shall be counted in the seed. » But this belongs properly to Isaac, who is not the son of the flesh, but of the promise, and is spiritual seed from the promise, not from the flesh.

VERS. 14.-- « Taking bread. » etc. [n: (ALC. EX BEDA, c. 21.)] When therefore Abraham cast out Agar, taking bread, he laid it upon her shoulders for this cause, etc. *as far as* [0136A] because certain of the people are to seek refuge under the shadow of the wood of the cross.

« He took bread and a bottle of water. » [var: Vulg. *tollens*, "taking" — the participle Migne's own lemma prints above] [n: (AUG. Q. in Gen. tom. III.)] It is asked: how did he lay upon her shoulders a boy so great? For he who, before Isaac was born, had been circumcised at thirteen years old, when Abraham was ninety-nine, and Isaac was born when his father was a hundred; and Ismael was playing with Isaac when Sara was moved, doubtless with a boy somewhat grown, who had already been weaned: assuredly he was more than sixteen years old when he was driven out with his mother. But granting that we take what is said of his playing with the little one as said by recapitulation, before Isaac was weaned; even so a boy of more than thirteen years could not be laid upon his mother's shoulders; [0136B] but it must be supplied, he gave over her boy, etc.

« He laid it on her shoulder. » [n: (HIER. in Q. Hebr.)] When Isaac was born, Ismael was thirteen years old, and after his weaning he is driven out with his mother. Among the Hebrews the opinion varies, some asserting the time of his weaning to have been appointed at the fifth year, others at the twelfth. We therefore, that we may choose the shorter age, reckon Ismael to have been cast out with his mother after eighteen years, and that it did not befit one already a youth to have sat upon his mother's neck. But that idiom of the Hebrew tongue is true, that every son is called an infant and a little one in comparison with his parents. At Rome too, even to this day, all sons are called infants.

VERS. 15-21.-- « She cast the boy. » etc. [n: (HIER.)] [0136C] That which follows: « And she cast the boy under one fir tree, and going away sat over against him afar off, as it were a bowshot. » [var: Vulg. *abjecit puerum subter unam arborum* … *seditque e regione procul quantum potest arcus jacere*, "under one of the trees" … "as far as a bow can shoot"] For she said: I shall not see the death of my little one, and she sat over against him; and at once it is joined. And the boy cried out and wept; and the Lord heard the voice of the boy from heaven, where he was, and the angel said to Agar: Let nothing move. For in the Hebrew this is written first: « I shall not see the death of my boy. » So it is read consequently, that Agar sat over against the boy, and lifted up her voice, and wept, and God heard the voice of the little one. For while the mother wept and awaited the death of her son, God heard the boy, whom he had promised should be for a great nation. For the mother bewails not her own death, but her son's.

[n: (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.)] It is asked how [0136D] the mother, etc., *as far as* as those walking, of whatever age, often do.

VERS. 17.-- « And he heard. » [n: (BED. et ISID. ubi supra.)] The boy cries out weeping, and God heard him; and, an angel showing it, she beholds the fountain, and it is said for those who are converted to Christ, and who, weeping for their sins, are heard, and with the eyes of the heart opened see the fountain of water, that is, Christ, who says Joan. VII « He that thirsteth, let him come to me and drink. » [var: Vulg. *Si quis sitit*, "If any man thirst"] The angel signifies Elias, through whom the people is to believe. Whence Malach. IV: « Behold I will send you Elias, who shall turn the hearts of the fathers unto the children. » [var: Vulg. Mal. 4:5-6, two verses joined: *Eliam prophetam, antequam veniat dies Domini* … *Et convertet cor patrum ad filios*]

« The angel of the Lord. » [var: Vulg. *angelus Dei*, "the angel of God"] [n: (ISID.)] In that Scripture pronounces him who speaks to Agar first an angel, afterwards the Lord, [0137A] he is to be believed to have been the Son of God, who spoke through the law and the prophets, who through obedience to the Father's will is called an angel, but according to nature is God.

VERS. 22.-- « At the same time. » [n: (AUG. ubi supra.)] It can be asked when Abraham made a compact with Abimelech, etc., *as far as* which she knew not.

VERS. 23-30.-- « Swear therefore, » etc. Often they who are without, drawn on by the converse of the faithful, desire to be joined to them and to be formed to better things by their examples, that they may take counsel both for themselves and for their neighbours' salvation.

VERS. 31, 32.-- « Therefore it was called, » etc. There is a twofold cause why it was called in Hebrew *Bersabee:* either because Abimelech received seven ewe-lambs from the hand [0137B] of Abraham. For seven are called *sabee,* or because they swore there, for *sabaa* signifies *an oath.* But if we read this cause above this name, it is said by prolepsis; as Bethel and Galgala, which, before they had these names, were called otherwise. It is to be noted from what goes before and from the present passage that Isaac was not born at the oak of Mambre ( *Alon* in Hebrew, in Latin *an oak* ) as is had in the Hebrew, but in Gerara, where Bersabee is a town even to this day, which province not long ago was called *Salutaris* from the division of the governors of Palestine. Whence Abraham dwelt in the land of the Palestinians many days.

VERS. 33, 34.-- « But Abraham, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] Abraham (as Stephen says) had not received an inheritance [0137C] there, nor the space of a foot. But the inheritance is to be understood which God was about to give freely, not bought with a price. And the space about the well is understood in reference to that space of purchase for which the seven ewe-lambs had been given, when Abimelech and Abraham swore to one another.



## CHAPTER XXII.

VERS. 1.-- « Which things after they were done, » etc. [n: (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.)] James says that God tempts no man; but in ordinary usage we say "to tempt" for that which is "to prove". James however speaks of that temptation by which every man is entangled in sin [ *Al.,* peccator]. Whence I Thess. I: « Lest perhaps he that tempteth should have tempted you. » For elsewhere it is written Deut. XIII: « [0137D] Your God tempteth you, that he may know whether you love him, that is, that he may make you to know. » [var: Vulg. *quia tentat vos Dominus Deus vester, ut palam fiat utrum diligatis eum an non*, "the Lord your God, … that it may appear openly whether you love him or not"] For a man knows not the powers of his own love, unless he learns it by experience.

VERS. 2.-- « Take thy son, » etc. [n: (ALC. in Gen., tom. I.)] Abraham was not bidden to kill his son at once, but to lead him for three days to the sacrifice, that by the length of the time the temptation might be increased. For through three days, with cares increasing, a father's bowels are tormented, and for a long space the father gazes upon his son, takes food with him, so many nights the boy hangs in his father's embrace, lies in his lap, and at every moment the grief of killing his son is heaped up in a father's love.

« Whom thou lovest, » etc. [n: (BEDA in Gen., c. 22.)] By the reminder of love and the recalling of the name the temptation [0138A] is heaped up, and a father's affection is stirred at the memory of the promise, because it was said: « In Isaac shall thy seed be called, » as though, if he were killed, the whole hope of the promise would be frustrated.

« Go into the land. » [n: (HIER. Q. Hebr., tom. III.)] The Hebrews say that this mountain is that in which the temple was afterwards built, on the threshing-floor of Orna the Jebusite. Whence II, Par. III: « They began to build the temple in the second month, on the second day of the month, in mount Moria, » [var: Vulg. *Et coepit Salomon aedificare domum Domini … Coepit autem aedificare mense secundo, anno quarto regni sui*, "Solomon began to build the house of the Lord … in the second month, in the fourth year of his reign"] which is for that cause interpreted *illuminating* and *shining,* because there is *debir* ( ) that is, the oracle of God, and the Spirit who teaches men the truth and inspires prophecies.

VERS. 3.-- « Leading his ass, » etc. [n: (ISID. in Gen., c. 18.)] The ass signifies the senseless folly of the Jews, which bore all the sacraments and [0138B] knew them not.

VERS. 4.-- « And on the third day. » [n: (HIER. ubi supra.)] It is to be noted that from Gerara as far as mount Moria, that is, the seat of the temple, is a journey of three days: and Abraham is said to have arrived there on the third day. Wrongly therefore do they think that Abraham dwelt at that time by the oak of Mambre, since to mount Moria there is scarce a full day's journey.
