The three days in which they came to the place of the sacrifice signify three ages, before the law, from Abraham to Moses; under the law, from Moses to John; thence to the Lord; and whatever remains, the third day is that of grace, in which third age the sacrifice of Christ was completed.

VERS. 5-8.-- « Stay here, » etc. [n: (ISID. ubi supra.)] Hear the Apostle: « Blindness in part happeneth [0138C] in Israel, » that is, « with the ass, » that « the fulness of the Gentiles might come in, » that is, « after we have adored. » When the sacrifice of the cross shall have been preached among the Gentiles, that is, « when the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in, we will return to you, » that is, « and so all Israel shall be saved. » The two servants left behind, and not brought to the place of the sacrifice, are the Jews who, living slavishly and savouring carnally, understood not the humility and passion of Christ. Two indeed, because they were to be divided into two parts, which came to pass when Solomon sinned, when the people was divided in respect of the kingdom, not by the error of impiety, to whom it is said Jer. III: « Israel the adversary, and Juda the prevaricator. » [var: Vulg. *aversatrix Israel, comparatione praevaricatricis Judae*, "the backslider Israel, in comparison of the treacherous Juda"]

« I and the boy, » etc. [n: (ALC.)] With undoubting mind he willed to slay his son. He is to be praised in the constancy [0138D] of offering and in the faith of raising up. For he knew most certainly that God cannot deceive, and that, though the boy were killed, yet the promise of God would remain safe. Whence the Apostle Hebr. XI: « By faith Abraham hesitated not, when he offered up his only-begotten, in whom he had received the promise, believing that God is able to raise up even from the dead. » [var: Vulg. Hbr 11:17,19 *Fide obtulit Abraham Isaac, cum tentaretur*, "By faith Abraham offered Isaac, when he was tried"; the opening words are not in Hebrews]

« Abraham said. » [n: (ISID. ubi supra.)] Abraham, leading his only son to be sacrificed, signifies God the Father. Abraham received a son in old age, but God grows not old, yet the promise concerning Christ had in a manner grown old when he was born. It began from Adam, when it was said: « They shall be two in one flesh » [n: (Gen. II)]; and it was completed in the sixth age of the world. [0139A] Sara's old age in the people of God, that is, in the multitude of the prophets, signifies this same thing, because in the end of the times Christ was born of the people of holy souls. Her barrenness signifies that in this world we are saved by hope, and that in Christ, as in Isaac, we are all born, which birth of the Church [ *Al.,* Ecclesia] in the end of the times not nature but grace brought forth. Abraham therefore signifies God the Father, Isaac Christ. For as Abraham offered his only and beloved son as a victim to God, so God the Father delivered up his only-begotten Son for us. And as Isaac carried the wood upon which he was to be laid, so Christ the cross on which he was to be fastened.

VERS. 9-11.-- « And when he had bound, » etc. [n: (ISID. ubi supra.)] Isaac with his feet bound is laid upon the altar, [0139B] and Christ is fastened to the cross. But that which was figured by Isaac was transferred to the ram, because Christ is a sheep. For he is a son, because born; a ram, because sacrificed. The ram sticks fast in the briars, the cross has horns. For if two pieces of wood be joined together, the shape of a cross is produced. Whence Habac. III: « Horns in his hands. » The ram therefore sticking fast by the horns is Christ crucified. But briars are thorns: wicked thorns, which hanged the Lord. For among the thorns of sinners he was hanged. Whence Jer.: « With the thorns of their sins this people hath encompassed me [n: (Jer. XXXVIII.)] » Others understand this ram bound in the briars to be Christ crowned with thorns before his immolation.

VERS. 12.-- « Thou hast not spared, » etc. [n: (AUG. ubi supra.)] Did Abraham then not spare his son [0139C] on account of the angel? But in the angel the Lord was figured, who is God and the angel of great counsel. For God was in the angel, and spoke in his person. This will appear the more in what follows, when it is said: « And the angel of the Lord called Abraham a second time from heaven, saying: By my own self have I sworn, saith the Lord. » For it is not easily found that Christ calls the Father Lord, especially before the incarnation. For according to the flesh this is congruously said, according to which it is said in the second psalm: « The Lord said to me, » etc. But that which is said in the same place, c. 9: « The Lord said to my Lord, » etc., is referred to the prophet who speaks; as that: « The Lord rained from the Lord, » that is, our Lord, namely the Son, from our Lord, namely the Father.

[0139D]VERS. 13.-- « The ram. » *[n: (ALC.)] Historically.* The ram which was sacrificed for Isaac, we have not imagined [it], but it is true. Therefore it is rather thought that an angel brought it from elsewhere, than that the Lord created it there out of the earth after the work of the six days.

« The ram among the briars, » etc. [n: (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.)] Aquila put *a briar* or *a thorn-brake,* etc., *as far as* which is so said in Hebrew.

VERS. 14-19.-- « And he called the name, » etc. [n: (HIER. ubi supra.)] For that which is had here, etc., *as far as* namely through the incarnation.

VERS. 20-24.-- « Melcha. » [n: (HIER. ubi supra.)] Of Melcha the wife of Nachor, etc., *as far as* and in that very name it is read in Isaias.



## CHAPTER XXIII.

[0140A]

VERS. 1-6.-- « And Sara lived, » etc. « And she died in the city of Arbee, » etc. [n: (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.)] Which is in the valley: this is not had in the authentic books, etc., *as far as* as is openly shown in the book of Josue.

VERS. 7-15.-- « Abraham rose up, » etc. [n: (AUG., Quaest. in Gen.)] It is asked how Abraham adored the people of the land, etc., *as far as* and therefore the adorer was to be corrected.

VERS. 16.-- « And when he had heard, » etc. [n: (HIER., de locis Hebr.)] In the Hebrew, as we have set it down, etc., *as far as* since he also is reproved who has received unwillingly.

VERS. 17-20.-- « And the field was confirmed, » etc. [0140B] [n: (GREG., lib. VI Moral., c. 25.)] He hints at a double sepulchre, etc., *as far as* or on account of the urgency of action puts off the things to be contemplated.

« The cave. » And the wooded field round about it is bought for the burial of Sara by the patriarch from the lords of the land for four hundred sicles of silver: because, for being free for wisdom and giving study to action, under the leaves of mystical words, as under fruitful trees, a quiet place is not otherwise acquired for preachers, unless by the evangelical doctrine, shining with the brightness of silver, eternal blessedness be urged upon earthly powers by them. Which is well figured by a hundred taken four times, because it is carried over from the left hand to the right.



## CHAPTER XXIV.

[0140C]

VERS. 1, 2.-- « And Abraham was old . . . . . And he said to his elder servant: Put thy hand, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] He bids him put his hand under his thigh, and swear by the God of heaven, because through that member his flesh was to come down who should be Abraham's son by his humanity, and divine by his divinity; as though he said: Touch my Son, and swear by my God. Yet he bids him put his hand not upon the thigh but under the thigh, because thence was to come down one who should be man, yet above men; whence it was not fitting that he should put his hand upon the thigh, because no flesh is above that flesh.

VERS. 3-8.-- « That thou take not. » [n: (GREG.)] *Allegorically.* A wife of the daughters of the Chanaanites is forbidden to Isaac, [0140D] because no souls of the reprobate are joined to Christ. A wife is bidden to be taken from his kindred, because the Church of the elect alone was to be wedded to Christ, whom the Only-begotten himself, by predestination and foreknowledge, did not already hold as a stranger. The servant who is sent to bring back the wife is the order of the prophets and apostles, and of all teachers; who, while they perform the word of preaching, become the providers, as it were, of every soul that is to be joined to the only-begotten Son.

VERS. 9.-- « He put therefore, » etc. [n: (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.)] The Hebrews hand down that in his sanctification, etc., *as far as* and that they should bear bravely the weight of tribulation, which the camels signify.

VERS. 10.-- « Of all his goods, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] [0141A] Because they who speak of the Lord show in themselves the riches of the virtues, that they may invite men so much the more quickly to follow God, as they show forth in themselves to their hearers the things they tell of.

VERS. 11-13.-- « By the well. » [n: (GREG.)] He stood by the fountain, and from a sentence fixed beforehand set forth which one was to be chosen, because holy preachers consider the flowing words. Whence they gather from what hearer, or from what hearers, they may take the confidence of certainty.

VERS. 14-16.-- « The maid therefore, » etc. [n: (AUG., Q. in Gen. )] It is to be asked what unlawful auguries differ from this petition, etc., *as far as* whereby it is forbidden to tempt God.

« Let down thy pitcher. » Humble thy worldly eloquence to draw up the prophetic word, that [0141B] I may bend thy will to my persuasion.

VERS. 17-21.-- « And the servant ran to meet her, and said: Give me a little, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] *Allegorically.* Every preacher thirsts for the soul of his hearer, therefore the servant asks, Rebecca gives the drink: because the Church of the elect satisfies the desire of preachers out of the strength of her faith. For she who confesses the God whom she hears, offers to the preacher the water of refreshment and cooling. And it is to be noted that she set down the pitcher, bending it from her shoulder, because that confession is pleasing which proceeds from a good work. Or she supplied water, because in that which she believed she did not remain empty. For at once she preached what she had heard, and by speaking brought forth many preachers out of herself. For the water in the pitcher is the knowledge of preaching in measure. For she studies not to be more wise than it behoveth to be wise. [0141C] The pitcher on the arm is the measure of preaching in work. And she gives drink not only to all his companions, but to the camels also, because the word of life is preached not only to the wise, but also to the foolish. Rom. I: « To the wise and to the unwise I am a debtor. » Or water is given even to the beasts, when it is ordered how care of the flesh is to be had, that it be not spent upon pleasure, and be not denied to necessity; whence Rom. XIII: « Make not provision for the flesh in its desires; » and Ephes. V: « For no man ever hated his own flesh. »

VERS. 22-24.-- « The man brought forth, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] *Beka* ( ), which is here put for a didrachma, is half an ounce: but *Schekel* ( ), which in Latin, though corruptly, is called *siclus*, is an ounce. [n: (GREG.)] Because the preacher [0141D] adorns the hearing of the Church by obedience, and its hands by work. The earrings are of two sicles, the bracelets of ten, because the first virtue of obedience lies in charity, which is the love of God and of the neighbour; and good work is perfected by the completion of the decalogue, so that when good things have begun to be done, no evil things may be done.

VERS. 25-28.-- « Straw also. » Because the Church, receiving spiritual things, renders earthly stipends to preachers; whence I Cor. IX: « If we have sown unto you spiritual things, it is a great thing if we reap your carnal things.

« A large room. » From the former people the Church showed that she had known the law of nature, and she received the preaching in the ample bosom of charity: for the teacher's spacious [0142A] dwelling is the breadth of charity in the hearer's heart; whence in the sixth of the second to the Corinthians: « Be not straitened in us, but in your own bowels; » [var: Vulg. *angustiamini autem in visceribus vestris*, "but you are straitened in your own bowels"] as if to say, enlarge the room of your mind for receiving doctrine, straiten it for thinking upon carnal things.

VERS. 29-32.-- « He had, » etc. Because carnal men joined to the faithful, while they behold spiritual gifts, hang in wonder, and if not so far as works, yet so far as the receiving of the faith they admit the preaching: for while they consider that good men shine with miracles, they refuse not the things they hear of eternity, although, not following the Church in their morals, they remain in carnal working.

« And he had. » Because there are carnal men joined to the faithful, by whose familiarity they sometimes come forth to hear [0142B] the word, and seeing the gifts of God bestowed on the faithful they marvel, and receive the word in the heart, which they are not at pains to fulfil in good morals.

VERS. 33-36.-- « I will not eat. » Because there are those who are ready to retain teachers out of temporal stipends: but they [the teachers] are unwilling to receive them [the stipends], unless they first obtain eternal things. For if they do not find fruit in souls, they scorn to take stipends for bodies; they wash not the feet, because they relieve the desires of labour with no consolation.

VERS. 37-41.-- « And he made me swear, » etc. [n: (AUG. ubi supra. )] The meaning is the same, the words not all, or the same, or so spoken; which is to be said on account of those who calumniate the evangelists because in some words they do not altogether agree, although the matter and the meaning be the same; [0142C] for one man wrote this book, who could have set down the things he said above, when Abraham gave the charge, or the rest, had he judged them to belong to the matter: since the truth of a narration does not require anything except that there be truth of things and of meanings, whereby the will (for the making known of which words are made) may sufficiently appear.

VERS. 42-52.-- « I came therefore to-day, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] In the Hebrew it is written, etc., *as far as* let the Jews therefore show *alma* set down anywhere where it sounds a young girl and not a virgin.

VERS. 53-58.-- « And bringing forth, » etc. [n: (GREG. ubi supra. )] After the servant obtained the cause of the marriage, etc., *as far as* « He gave them to Rebecca. » Rebecca is not adorned with the gold of Bathuel, because the ornaments of a barbarian and unskilled man are not worthy. From the house of Abraham she receives ornaments, [0142D] because patience is adorned from the house of wisdom; from Abraham's servant she receives earrings, that is, golden words in the ears; and bracelets, that is, golden works in the hands, and other ornaments; by which are figured the diverse kinds of the virtues.

VERS. 59-60.-- « And her nurse. » It was seemly that a virgin setting out to her wedding without her parents should be cherished with the solace of a nurse.

VERS. 61.-- « Rebecca therefore, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] Because the Church has with her souls of lesser merit, who follow her in their morals: but they are not advanced to the couch of contemplation. She had also certain servants in her company, because with the prophets there were men who lived well, even though they had not the spirit of prophecy: [0143A] and with the apostles and teachers, men who held to their life, and yet did not preach.

« Who in haste, » etc. The servant returns in haste to his master, because preachers, when they obtain the life of their hearers, present it to God, of whose gift they have it, not to themselves.

VERS. 62.-- « At that time Isaac, » etc. [n: (GREG. ubi supra. )] The Living and Seeing One is the Lord, etc., *as far as* he bore now a crown of thorns, now a cross.

« He dwelt, » etc. [n: (PATERIUS in Gen. ex dictis GREG.)] Isaac, when Rebecca came, was dwelling in the south country, because Christ, when the Church came to him, abode in the minds of those whom not the cold of the body but the fervour of charity held: such as were Anna, Simeon, and some of the people of the Jews.

[0143B]VERS. 63.-- « And he was gone forth, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] By the south country he signifies Gerara, whence he had been brought to be sacrificed. He went forth, that he might exercise himself in the field. In the Hebrew it is read: « Isaac went forth to speak into the field, the evening being now come on; » where it is signified that the Lord prayed alone upon the mountain, and that Isaac, who was a type of the Lord, went forth from the house at the ninth hour, or before sunset, to prayer as a just man, and offered spiritual victims to God.

[n: (GREG. ubi supra. )] The field is the world, into which Christ went forth, because he vouchsafed to appear visibly.

Whence Habac. III: « Thou wentest forth for the salvation of thy people. » Isaac went out into the field to meditate, because Christ, holding forth the form of humility, showed the exercise of long-suffering, [0143C] of passion, and examples of patience, as trained young men are wont to meditate in the use of arms. The meditation of arms is the frequenting of the passion. For he who bore stripes, spittings, the lance and the cross, suffered his passion to be frequented in himself even unto death. We call the passions arms, because through them we are freed from our adversaries; whence Luc. XXI: « In your patience you shall possess your souls. »

« The day being now far spent, etc. Because he took upon him the exercises of the passions near the end of the world; whence psal. CXL: The lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice. »

VERS. 64.-- « She came down from the camel, » etc. [n: (GREG., lib. I, Moral., c. 14.)] Rebecca is brought to Isaac upon a camel, etc., *as far as* upon the crooked backs of camels they carry riches.

[0143D]VERS. 65, 66.-- « Who is that man, » etc. [n: (GREG. apud Paterium. )] Rebecca, having seen Isaac, learns who he is by asking the servant: because the Church, by the sayings of the prophets and apostles, understands what she ought to believe concerning Christ. And she straightway covered herself with a cloak, because the more she penetrates the mysteries of Christ, the more she is confounded at her past life. Whence she strives to cover her infirmity with a cloak; and she who before was carried freely upon the camel, coming down is covered with shame, to whom it is said as it were more shamefacedly: What fruit therefore hadst thou then in those things of which thou art now ashamed?

« But she, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Another edition, « teristrum, » which even now is a kind of Arabian garment wherewith the women of that province are veiled.

[0144A]VERS. 67.-- « Who brought her in, » etc. [n: (GREG. ut supra. )] Isaac brought her into his mother's tent, and took her to wife, because in the place of the Synagogue, of which Christ was born, he loves the Church: that she who by kindred, that is, by predestination, had been nearest, may by love be joined as his wife.



## CHAPTER XXIV. (cont.)

« And he loved her so much, » etc. [n: (GREG. ut supra. )] Because from the gain of the Church Christ wiped away the sorrow that came of the loss of the Synagogue, because while the Church, coming out of the Gentile world, is brought to the couch of contemplation, Judaea is held for nothing. Isaac too is called *laughter,* and Rebecca *patience*: and laughter comes of gladness, patience of tribulation. And although the Church be now suspended in the contemplation of heavenly joy, yet she has in herself somewhat whereat she may grieve [0144B] for the weight of the flesh. But Isaac and Rebecca are joined together, that is, laughter and patience, according to that of Rom. XII: « Rejoicing in hope, patient in tribulation, » that prosperous things may gladden out of contemplation, adverse things disturb out of tribulation.



## CHAPTER XXV.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « But Abraham took another wife, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] *Allegorically.* Sara being dead, Abraham took Cetura. Not on account of incontinence, since he was already of great age: nor on account of the procuring of children, since seed as the stars of heaven had been promised him from Isaac. But as Agar and Ismael figured the carnal men of the Old Testament, so Cetura and her sons the heretics, who [0144C] deem themselves to belong to the New Testament. But both were concubines, Sara alone the wife; whence Cant. VI: « One is my dove, » etc.

*Allegorically.* After the assumption of the Church many souls deceived by heretical depravity clave to Christ, and these brought forth not catholics but schismatics, who divided the unity of the Church: whom the sons of Cetura signify, who are numbered below.

[n: (HIER., in Q. Hebr. )] Cetura, in Hebrew, *joined,* etc., *as far as* for the more a man is wearied in the flesh, the more robust is he in spirit and the fitter for the embraces of wisdom.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « The sons of Dadan were Assurim, and Lathusim, and Laomim. But of Madian was born Epha and Opher and Henoc and Abida. » [n: (HIER.)] They think that Assurim is to be rendered *merchants,* etc., *as far as* [0144D] and that the regions of India were occupied by very many of the sons of Abraham by Cetura.

VERS. 5-7.-- « And Abraham gave, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] Because gifts are given to the sons of the concubines, that is, of carnal men; but heretics, or Jews, do not attain to the promised kingdom, because they follow carnal gains; Isaac alone is the heir, because not the children of the flesh are counted in the seed, but of the promise.

VERS. 8-11.-- « And decaying he died in a good old age, and of advanced years, and full of days, and was gathered to his people. And they buried him, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] it is wrongly added in the LXX: « Abraham decaying died, » because it does not befit Abraham to decay or to be diminished. That also [0145A] which we have set down « in a good old age an old man, » and which is put more fully in the Greek codices, « full of days; » which, though it seem to expound the sense, in that he died full of light and of the works of day, yet makes the more for the anagogy if it be simply set down « full. »

[n: ( Glossa Graeca. )] Behold, Moses knew the life of the world to come, for no man can be added to those who are not.

VERS. 12.-- « These are the generations, » etc. These peoples are reckoned before the sons of Isaac, who, although they be of the seed of Abraham, are yet not reckoned in the seed, because that which is animal is first, then that which is spiritual. Whence after the reckoning of the carnal, the genealogy of Isaac is set down, of whom the promise was made, and the succession of the elect to be propagated.

[0145B]VERS. 13-15.-- « And these are the names of his sons in their titles and generations. The firstborn of Ismael Nabajoth, then Cedar, and Abel, » [var: Vulg. *Adbeel*] etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Twelve sons are born to Ismael, the firstborn Nabajoth, from whom all the region from the Euphrates to the Red Sea is called Nabathea, which is a part of Arabia. For their families, towns, villages and tribes are celebrated by the naming of these. From one of these, Cedar in the desert, and Duma another region, and Thema to the south, and Chedma, otherwise Chethma, to the eastern quarter, is named. As to what we read at the end of the chapter according to the LXX, « he dwelt over against the face of all his brethren, » it is truer which we have set down, « he fell asleep before all his brethren, » that is, he died in the hands of his sons, his children [0145C] yet surviving. For sons are called brethren; whence Jacob: « Let it be set before my brethren and thy brethren, and let them judge between us. » [var: Vulg. Gn 31:37 *pone hic … et judicent inter me et te*, "put it here … and let them judge between me and thee"] For it is not to be believed that Jacob, apart from his own children, had any brethren with him.

VERS. 16, 17.-- « Twelve princes of their tribes. And the years of the life of Ismael were, » etc. The number twelve, as it is consecrated in the apostles and in apostolic men, so is it execrable in the reprobate against the faith of the Trinity and the Gospel spread abroad through the four quarters of the world.

VERS. 18, 19.-- « And he dwelt from Hevila as far as Sur, which looketh towards Egypt, » etc. The region which Phison went about was so called from Hevila, the grandson of Noe, and it is a wilderness over against the face of Egypt. Sur also [0145D] is a wilderness between Cades and Barad, stretching the desert as far as the Red Sea and the borders of Egypt.

VERS. 20.-- « From Mesopotamia the sister of Laban. » A barren region, which is so called in Greek because it lies in the middle between two rivers, the Tigris and the Euphrates. For μέσος is said *middle,* ποταμὸς *a river*; hence hippopotamus, that is, a river horse.

VERS. 21, 22.-- « And he besought . . . but the little ones struggled in her womb, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] For the stirring or the collision the LXX put « they played » or « they kicked back: » Aquila, « the sons fought in her womb. » Symmachus, « they were borne upon the surface after the likeness of a ship. » [n: (GREG., Dial. I, 8.)] The things which the saints [0146A] effect by praying, etc., *as far as* for he in whom God had predestined to multiply the seed of Abraham obtained by prayer that he might have sons.

« Who heard her. » [var: Vulg. *eum*, "him" — Isaac, who is the one that prayed] [n: (CHRYSOST.)] Think not that he obtained forthwith and at once what he desired: for he continued twenty years praying and beseeching God. For as he taught us how many years he had when he took Rebecca (namely forty), so he assigned to us of how many years Isaac was when sons were born to him. « He was sixty years old, he says, when the little ones were born. »

« And she went, » etc., [n: (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.)] It is asked: whither did she go, since there were as yet no prophets or priests of the tabernacle or of the temple of the Lord? Perhaps to the place where Abraham set up an altar. [0146B] But how answers were given there, Scripture is silent; was it through some priest? And it is a wonder, if there was one, that he was not named, and that no mention of priests was made. Perhaps there, when they had laid their desires before God in prayer, they slept, that they might be admonished through a dream: or Melchisedech was yet living, whose excellence was so great that it was doubted whether he were a man or an angel. Or perhaps even at that time there were men of God, in whom God could be consulted. Whichever of these, or perhaps somewhat else, it was. Rebecca however according to the Scripture consulted the Lord, and deserved his answer.

VERS. 23.-- « Who answering said: Two nations, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] By historical propriety the answer is found fulfilled, when for Israel's sake, that is, Jacob's, [0146C] the younger son overcame the Idumeans, that is, the nation which Esau propagated, and made them tributaries through David; which they were for a long time, until king Joram [n: (IV Reg. VIII)]. For then the Idumeans rebelled, and shook the yoke from their neck, as Isaac had prophesied when he blessed the younger before the elder, and said this same thing to the elder, when he afterwards blessed him.

VERS. 24-26.-- « The time was now come, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] Where we have put « hairy », in the Hebrew there stands *seyr* ( ); whence Esau, as we read elsewhere, was called *seyr,* that is, hairy.

« Forthwith the other, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] That this was done figuratively is known even to the Jews who believe not: how the people of the Church overcame the people of the Synagogue, and the people of the Jews, the greater in time, served [0146D] the lesser people of the Christians.

[n: (BED. in hoc cap., tom. II.)] In individual men also this may be said. « Two nations and two peoples » are within us, namely of the vices and of the virtues; the one lesser, the other greater: for the evil are more than the good, and the vices more than the virtues. Yet the people overcomes the people, « and the elder shall serve the younger, » namely the flesh the spirit, and the vices the virtues. Esau goes before, red, and all hairy as a skin: then comes forth Jacob holding his brother's heel; for the former people, polluted with the blood of the prophets and of Christ, was encompassed with the squalor of sin and of wickedness: whose heel the younger held, because the lesser people overcame the greater.

VERS. 27, 28.-- « Esau became, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] [0147A] It signifies the life of those who, poured out upon outward pleasures, follow the flesh. He is also called a husbandman, because lovers of the world cultivate outward things the more, the more they leave their own inward things untilled.

« Simple. » [n: (GREG.)] Because simple men shun to be scattered abroad by outward cares, and abide in thought and in the dwelling of conscience. For he dwells in tents who binds himself within the secret places of the mind, lest, while he gapes outwards after many things, he depart from himself, estranged in thought.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « And Jacob boiled, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] *Red* or *tawny:* in Hebrew it is called *Edom*: and from this, that Esau sold his birthright for red food, he received the name of the tawny, that is, of *Edom*.

VERS. 31, 32.-- « To whom Jacob said, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] [0147B] That Esau sold his birthright to his younger brother for food, and was afterwards deprived of the paternal blessing promised him, signifies the Jewish people: who in Exodus is called the firstborn son of God, and for the gains of the world not only lost the honour of his primacy, but also deserved not to obtain the prepared reward of the heavenly kingdom. Whence by way of reproach it is said Matth. XII: « The kingdom of God shall be taken away from you and shall be given to the Gentiles, » [var: Vulg. Mt 21:43 *dabitur genti facienti fructus ejus*, "shall be given to a nation yielding the fruits thereof"] etc. But the birthright was a priestly garment, clothed in which the elder-born offered victims to God with their father's blessing, as pontiffs: of which gift, through desire of earthly love, the Jews were bereft, together with the glory of the kingdom to come.

VERS. 33, 34.-- « Esau swore, » etc. [n: (GREG., lib. XXX, Moral., c. 13.)] But it is to be known that the vice of gluttony tempts us in five [0147C] ways, etc., *as far as* and not the things which the lust of eating suggests.



## CHAPTER XXVI.

VERS. 1-7.-- « Now a famine having arisen upon the land, » etc. [n: (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.)] It is asked when this was done, that Isaac went into Gerara, etc., *as far as* that we may understand that temporal things can neither be given, nor ought to prosper, save from the one God.

VERS. 8-11.-- « And when they had passed through, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] From the time of Christ until the times of Constantine the faith was preached not in the assembly of the peoples, but as it were through hiding-places, and the Christians were gathered together in secret: at length the princes of the world, being now Christians, seeing through the miracles of Christ his majesty tempered [0147D] to human infirmity, that they might be joined together and be « two in one flesh, » permitted the sacrament of Christ and the Church to be preached now openly, and grieved that it had long lain hidden.

« He saw him sporting, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] What it means, in the sacrament of Christ and the Church, etc., *as far as* « that he might dwell in us. »

VERS. 12, 13.-- « Now Isaac sowed in that land, » [var: Vulg. Gen. 26:12 *Sevit*, "planted"] etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Although Isaac sowed in a foreign land, I do not think that so great a fruitfulness of barley was his. Whence I judge it better what is had in the Hebrew, and what Aquila translated: « He found in that year a hundredfold reckoned. » But Scripture, keeping silence as to the kind of crops which yielded a hundredfold, seems [0148A] to have shown in him the multiplication of all virtues.

VERS. 14-16.-- « For these things envying, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] Isaac, Christ, who first wills to dig wells, etc., *as far as* « we shall go. »

« The wells they also stopped up, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] *Morally.* Often, when we are intent upon the divine words, we endure the more grievously the snares of demons; which sprinkle upon our mind the dust of earthly thoughts, that they may darken the eyes of our purpose from the light of the inward vision. For we dig wells when we penetrate the deep things of Scripture, which the Allophyli secretly fill up: because they thrust in earthly thoughts and take away the water of divine knowledge, whence in psalm CXVIII: « Depart from me, O malignant ones, and I will search the commandments of the Lord. »

[0148B]VERS. 17.-- « And he departing, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] And Isaac went thence, and came into the valley of Gerara, and dwelt there. In place of *valle, torrentem* they have in the Hebrew. For never, after he was made great, could Isaac dwell in a valley, but in a torrent. Of which it is written in psalm CIX: « He shall drink of the torrent in the way. » Of which Elias drank in the time of famine; but because he was not perfect as Christ, the torrent dried up for him. But Christ also was delivered up in the torrent, dedicating our regeneration and the sacrament of baptism.

VERS. 18, 19.-- « He dug wells elsewhere, » etc. [n: (ISID. ex Greg.)] Isaac dug wells among a foreign nation: wherein we are admonished that in this pilgrimage we penetrate the deep things of our thoughts, and that, until the water of true understanding return, the hand of inquiry grow not sluggish in [0148C] draining out the earthly things of the heart. The Allophyli, lying in wait, fill up the wells, because unclean spirits, when they behold us digging earnestly, thrust the vexations of temptations into our thoughts.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « Against the shepherds, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Aquila and Symmachus translated « adverse and contrary, » but in the Hebrew there is « Satana; » whence it is plain that Satan is interpreted *contrary*.

VERS. 22-25.-- « Breadth. » In the Hebrew *Rehobot* ( ) [ed: Migne prints a Hebrew word within these parentheses; our digitized source has lost it and left them empty. Recovery requires the plate.], *breadths.* Whence that is proved which was said above in chapter X: « He himself built Ninive » and *rabooth,* that is, the streets thereof.

« Now he hath enlarged, » etc. Isaac was enlarged, and filled the whole earth with the knowledge of the Trinity: and in the whole [0148D] world he set the breadth of the Church, being nevertheless first known in Judea, and named in Israel: afterwards « their sound went forth into all the earth [n: (Psal. XVIII)], » that is, the sound of the apostles, who throughout the universal world dug wells, and by water showed [it] to all, baptizing all nations in the name of the Father, and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost [n: (Matth. XVIII)]. » The wells of Abraham, which he opened, he called by the same names as his father: for Moses and the prophets are called among us by their own names, nor are the names of these wells changed.

VERS. 26-31.-- « To which place, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Abimelech has not always peace with Isaac, etc., *as far as* and there are many other things in which the philosophers disagree with us, or agree.

[0149A]In place of « Ochozath, » *pronubo,* there is in the Hebrew « the company of his friends, » so that it may signify both the man and the crowd of friends which had come with the king: among whom was Phicol, the prince of his army.

« Abimelech, and Ochozath, » etc. [n: (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.)] Two came with Abimelech: Ochozath, his son-in-law, etc., *as far as* he is a debtor to the wise and to the unwise.

VERS. 32-35.-- « But behold, they came. » [n: (HIER.)] I know not how in the LXX « and the servants of Isaac came, telling him of the well which they had dug, and they said to him: We have not found water. And he called the name of it, Oath. » For what etymology there is, that it should for this cause be called Oath, etc., *as far as* it does not agree [0149B] that Isaac should not find water.



## CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Now Isaac grew old, » etc. *[n: (HIPP. mart., ex Hieron., epist. ad Damasum, tom. I.)] Mystically.* Isaac bears the image of God the Father, Rebecca of the Holy Ghost, Esau of the former people and of the devil, Jacob of the Church and of Christ. The old age of Isaac signifies the consummation of the world; his darkening eyes, that faith has perished from the world, and that the light of religion has been neglected. Because he is called the elder son, it is the receiving of the law of the Jews. Because the father loved his meats and his hunting: they are men saved from error, whom every just man hunts by his teaching. The word of God, the blessing of the promise, and the hope of the kingdom to come, in which with Christ [0149C] they are to reign, and to celebrate the true sabbath. Rebecca, full of the Holy Ghost, knowing what she had heard before she bore, that « the elder shall serve the younger: » she bears the form of the Holy Ghost, who, what she knew was to be in Christ, beforehand meditated in Jacob, speaks to the younger son. « Go to the flock and bring me two of the best kids; » prefiguring the fleshly coming of the Saviour, in which he should set free those who were held liable to sins. For kids everywhere signify sinners. Two are commanded to be brought, that the taking up of the two peoples might be signified. That is, of a tender and human, that is, of a teachable and innocent soul. The robe of Esau is the faith, that is, the Scriptures of the Hebrews which were first given to them, with which the Gentile people was afterwards clothed; the skins put about [0149D] his arms, the sins of both peoples, which Christ, in the stretching out of his hands, fastened with himself to the cross: for he in his own body bore not his own, but others' sins.

VERS. 2.-- « And he called Esau. » [n: (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.)] Esau a hunter, and a husbandman, but Jacob, a simple man, dwelt in tents. Esau signifies the Hebrew people, Jacob the Gentile people. But Isaac [signifies] God, who made both peoples sons to himself, the one by the law, the other by faith. Esau was a husbandman, because the Jew served God for earthly things, to whom it was not said that he should be poor in spirit, meek, peaceable, merciful, suffering persecution, that he might have a plentiful reward in the heavens; but: « If [0150A] you keep my commandments, I will give you rains in their seasons, » etc., which pertain to the delights of the flesh. He was also a hunter, because by the pouring out of the blood of rams and of calves, and of goats, he appeased God. « Jacob, a simple man, dwelt in tents, » because the elect of the Gentiles studied to please God by faith and by will. By the tents we understand the divers Churches throughout the world, or the divers orders

VERS. 3-5.-- « Take thy arms, » etc. Isaac loved Esau, because he fed upon his hunting. Rebecca loved Jacob, Isaac [signifies] God; but Rebecca figures the foreknowledge of God, because God loved the people of the Jews, in that he was continually appeased by their oblations. But foreknowing that the people of the Gentiles would be far better, by the grace of his foreknowledge [0150B] he loved them more abundantly. While Esau tarried at the hunt, that he might set meats from the wild beasts before his father and be blessed, the mother brought Jacob to his father to receive the blessing: because the Jews still tarry in the things of the law, and come late to the faith of Christ, the mother, grace, offered the Gentiles, eagerly taking up the faith, to receive the blessing owed to the Jews. But in what manner they earned the gift of the blessing, the works of Jacob bear witness, which he did in order to receive the blessing.

VERS. 6-13. « She said to her son Jacob: I have heard thy father speaking, » etc. *Allegorically.* By the two kids we may understand the confession of sins of the Gentile people and its penance. The best kids, because the confession of sins and penance is very acceptable to God. [0150C] Whence Luke XV: « There shall be joy in heaven upon one sinner doing penance, » etc. *Historically.* By the one sinner he understands the people of the Gentiles, by the ninety-nine just, the Jews, who seemed to themselves just in the observance of the law. Whence Luke XVI: « For what cause do you justify yourselves before men, » [var: Vulg. Luc. 16:15 *Vos estis qui justificatis vos coram hominibus*, "You are they who justify yourselves before men" — a statement, not a question] etc.; who are set down in an imperfect number, because the law brought no one to perfection.

VERS. 14.-- « She prepared meats, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] *Mystically.* By the delightful meats which are prepared for Isaac from the kids, we understand the works of penance, etc., *as far as* it often brings past sins before the eyes of the mind.

VERS. 15-19.-- « And with the garments of Esau, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] In this place the Hebrews hand down that the firstborn discharged [0150D] the office of priests, and had a priestly vestment, clothed wherein they offered victims, before Aaron was chosen unto the priesthood.

VERS. 20-24.-- « How, said he, couldst thou find it so quickly, my son? » [n: (HIPPOLYT.)] Isaac asks of Jacob why he had come so quickly, marvelling, that is, at the swift faith of believers. Delightful meats are offered, because a victim pleasing to God is the salvation of sinners. Because after the eating the blessing follows, and he enjoys the odour thereof, he openly declares the power of the resurrection and of the kingdom. « Behold the smell of my son as the smell of a full field. » This is the blessing. With the odour of the name of Christ, as a field, the world is filled. The blessing is of the dew of heaven, that is, the rain of the divine word; and of the fatness of the earth, [0151A] that is, the gathering together of peoples; of the multitude of corn and wine, this is the multitude which he gathers from the sacrament of his body and blood. Peoples serve him, that is, the converted Gentiles. The tribes of the peoples adore him, believers converted through circumcision. He is the Lord of his brethren, that is, of the Jews. The sons of his mother adore him, because according to the flesh he was born of her: he that shall curse him, shall be cursed; and he that shall bless him, shall be blessed. Christ is blessed from the mouth of a people ignorant of the Father, that is, is truly spoken of, but by the Jews another is thought to be blessed, who is looked for by them that err.

VERS. 25, 26.-- « Which when he had eaten, being offered him, » etc. *Mystically.* After Isaac had taken food, Jacob offered him wine: so by the food the works of the active life [0151B] are signified; by the wine, which estranges the mind, the contemplative is understood, which makes the minds of the elect estranged from the love of the world. After the food and the drink. Jacob is blessed, because they receive the heavenly blessing from God who study to appease him unto the end by good works.

VERS. 27.-- « Blessing him, he said, » etc. [n: (GREG., hom. 6 in Ezech.)] The one is sent to hunt, etc., *as far as* which the elder, going forth abroad, left within.

[n: (GREG., ibid.)] Isaac, with darkened eyes, blessed his son Jacob not knowing him; the things that were to come he foresaw, and who stood present he knew not. For it is certain that the Jews, who had fully learned the law, knew the coming of Christ. Whence in Matthew II, Herod inquires of [0151C] the chief priests where Christ should be born; and they answered him forthwith, « In Bethlehem. » Therefore they had known him before, whom, at the time of the passion, while they despised him, they knew not; whose earlier knowledge, and later ignorance, is signified by the darkening of Isaac; who, while he blessed Jacob, foresaw what should befall him in time to come, but knew not that he stood present. So the people of the Jews received the mysteries of prophecy, but held their eyes blind in contemplation, because they saw not him present, of whom they foresaw many things in time to come. For they were not able to discern him set before them, the power of whose coming they had long before announced.

[n: (GREG., hom. 10 in Ezech., tom. II.)] Isaac knows not the son whom he blessed, etc., *as far as* and because through some it rises up into contemplation, through some it grows fat in [0151D] the works of the active life. There follows:

VERS. 28, 29.-- « God give thee of the dew of heaven, » etc. Dew falls from above, and finely, and we receive of the dew of heaven as often as, by the infusion of inward contemplation, we see something finely of the things above. But when we do good works through the body, we are enriched from the fatness of the earth.

« Abundance of corn, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] This is the multitude which he gathers from the sacrament of his body and blood.

VERS. 30-32.-- « Esau came, » etc. Late does Esau return to his father, because the former people shall be converted at the end. Whence in psalm LVIII: « They shall return at evening, and shall suffer hunger like dogs, and shall go round about [0152A] the city. » And elsewhere: « They opened their mouths, as one that eateth in secret. »

VERS. 33-35. « Isaac was affrighted. » When the elder son sought again the promised blessing, Isaac was affrighted, and knew that he had blessed the one for the other; nor is he indignant, the sacrament being known, but he confirmed the blessing, saying: « And I have blessed him, and he is blessed. » [var: Vulg. Gen. 27:33 *benedixique ei, et erit benedictus*, "and I have blessed him, and he shall be blessed"] This is the first blessing of Isaac, given to the lesser people of the Christians. But neither is the elder son altogether despised, because, when the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in, then all Israel shall be saved. Of which second blessing there is a prophecy. « In the fatness of the earth, and in the dew of heaven from above shall be thy blessing. « In the fatness [sic: *pinguedidine*] of the earth, » that is, in the fruitfulness of things, and in the power of the kingdom, which was in that people; « and in the dew of heaven, » [0152B] the eloquences of God. For to them were entrusted the eloquences of God, and the testaments of the law. « Thou shalt live by the sword: » because that people, given over to blood, wrought slaughter upon Christ and the prophets, « And thou shalt serve thy younger brother, » [var: Vulg. Gen. 27:40 *et fratri tuo servies*, without *minori*] that is, the Christian people. « And the time shall come, when thou shalt shake off and loose his yoke from thy neck; » that is, when, converted through the knowledge of faith and the grace of Christ, thou shalt have laid down the burden of the law; when then thou shalt no longer serve the younger, but through faith shalt be called brother.

« Isaac was affrighted, » etc. So that a certain alienation of mind should follow, which is called ecstasy, which is wont to come to pass in the revelations of great things. Whence it is to be understood that Isaac was spiritually admonished to confirm the blessing upon Jacob, with whom he ought rather [0152C] to have been angry, because he deceived his father. In Adam also an ecstasy went forth, before he said: « They shall be two in one flesh [n: (Gen. II)], » which is « a great sacrament in Christ and in the Church. »

« And I have blessed him, » etc. [n: (ALC.)] If the will of a just man is good, how is it that Isaac blessed not Esau whom he wished, but Jacob whom he wished not? The will of a just man, as far as pertains to conscience, is good: but God alone judges of things to come. Isaac after the manner of men thought that the elder son was to be blessed: but God, who is the knower of hidden things, showed the younger worthy of the blessing, that he might show the blessing to be not of man, but of God: therefore in Numbers it is said to Moses and Aaron, the priests: « Put my name upon the children of Israel. I the Lord [0152D] will bless them [n: (Num. VI)]. » [var: Vulg. Num. 6:27 *Invocabuntque nomen meum super filios Israel, et ego benedicam eis*, "And they shall invoke my name upon the children of Israel, and I will bless them"] It belongs to the priest to bless, to God to grant the effect. But when Isaac understood through the spirit of prophecy that the blessing was destined to the younger, he said, « I have blessed him, and he shall be blessed. »

VERS. 36-39.-- « Rightly is his name called Jacob: for he hath supplanted me, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] Jacob is interpreted *a supplanter*. Therefore from this, that he had before deceived his brother, he alluded to the name, who was for this cause called Jacob before, because at his birth he took hold of his brother's heel.

VERS. 40.-- « And thou shalt serve thy brother, » etc. Because the Idumeans were tributaries to Israel, who is called Jacob.



## CHAPTER XXVII. (cont.)

« And the time shall come, when thou shalt shake off and loose his yoke. » , etc. This was fulfilled when the [0153A] Idumeans rebelled that they might not be under Juda. « And the time shall come, » etc. As if to say: The Jewish people shall at the end be set free from the servitude of sin, so that when « the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in, all Israel shall be saved [n: (Rom. XI)]. »

VERS. 41.-- « And he said in his heart. » Since Scripture says that Esau treated in his heart of his brother's death, how or by whom were these things told to Rebecca? But it is given to be understood that all things were divinely revealed to her. Because of the incurable hatred of the Jews, by the spirit of counsel it was commanded to the apostles that, Judea being left, they should pass over to the Gentiles.

« The days of mourning shall come, » etc. For he would not do this while his father lived, lest he should incur his hatred, and be cursed by him.

[n: (DIOD.)] But that which is written, « Esau said in [0153B] his heart, » signifies that Esau longed for his brother's death not with a sudden anger, but with a perfected consent of the mind.

VERS. 42.-- « These things were told to Rebecca, » etc. Here consequently is set down the mother's meeting of it, when it is said, « These things were told. » From which it is plain that Esau did not so hide the aforesaid contrivance in his heart, but that it appeared outwardly in signs and in words.

« Who sending and calling Jacob, » etc. That she might induce him to give place to wrath and to withdraw for a time. There follows:

« Why shall I be deprived of both my sons? » etc. As if she should say: If he assail thee, it may be that you shall both be slain; or if one remain, it will behove that he be a fugitive from me.

[0153C] « Rebecca also said, » etc. Because Jacob could not fittingly withdraw without his father's knowledge, therefore Rebecca turned herself to inducing Isaac to send Jacob his son to Laban, that he might there take a wife, saying.

« I am weary, » etc. That is, because of the wives of Esau: but she did not tell Isaac himself another cause, namely of Esau's contrivance against the life of Jacob, lest in his old age he should be too much afflicted.

« If Jacob take a wife, » etc. As if she should say: If thou lovest my life, send him to the house of my brother, that he may there take a wife.

## CHAPTER XXVIII.

VERS. 1-9.-- « Isaac therefore called, » etc. *(AUG., [0153D] Q. in Gen.)* What the Latin codices have: « Go into Mesopotamia, into the house of Bathuel, » etc., the Greeks have: « Flee into Mesopotamia, » etc., so that it may be understood that Isaac also understood that Esau had thought upon his brother's death.

« And setting out. » [n: (ISID.)] Jacob, fleeing the wiles of his brother, leaving his father's house and his parents, goes into a far country, that he may take a wife. In like manner Christ, leaving his parents according to the flesh, that is, the Jews, and his country, that is, Jerusalem, and all the regions of Judea, went away to the Gentiles, that he might take to himself the Church, according to that of Osee II: « I will call that which is not my people, » etc.

VERS. 10.-- « Jacob therefore going forth, » etc. *(GREG., [0154A] lib. V Moral., c. 11, tom. I.)* To sleep in the way, etc., *as far as* « for the Lord is sweet. »

VERS. 11.-- « He took of the stones, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] The sleeping of Jacob in the way, the death of Christ on the cross. The stone set under his head is Christ according to his humanity, whom Jacob anointed, that Christ might be signified, for χρίστος in Greek is *anointed* in Latin. But the head is Christ God. The stone therefore set under the head of Jacob signifies the humanity of Christ joined to the God who assumed it. « For the head of the man is Christ, the head of Christ is God [n: (I Cor. XI.)]

VERS. 12.-- « And he saw in his sleep, » etc. [n: (GREG., Regist. l. I, ep. 24.)] Jacob signifies preachers, who not only, by contemplating, [0154B] seek after the head of the Church, that is, Christ, but descend by compassion to his members: which the angels ascending and descending show forth. Hence Moses frequently enters the tabernacle, and goes out; and he who within is caught up to contemplation, without is pressed by the business of the weak; within he considers hidden things, without he bears the burdens of the carnal. He who in doubtful matters ever runs back to the tabernacle, consults the Lord before the ark of the testament, giving an example to rulers, that when they are in doubt without as to what they should ordain, they may return to the mind as to a tabernacle, and, as it were before the ark of the testament, may consult the Lord concerning the things they doubt of, seeking within themselves the pages of the sacred eloquence.

*Morally.* To sleep in the way is, in the way of this world, to rest from the hindrance of worldly actions. [0154C] Jacob sleeping saw angels: because they behold divine things who close their eyes from the desire of temporal things: which, being well closed, the devil opened, saying: « In whatsoever day you shall eat, your eyes shall be opened [n: (Gen. III)]. »

« He saw in his sleep, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] The angels ascending and descending by the ladder are the evangelists and the preachers of Christ, ascending to the understanding of the Divinity; passing beyond every creature, that they may find in the beginning the Word with God, by whom all things were made. Descending, that they may find him « made of a woman, made under the law, » etc. In that ladder also from earth to heaven, from flesh to spirit: because in it the carnal, by advancing, as it were by ascending, become spiritual. To whom, to be nourished with milk, [0154D] the spiritual descend, because they cannot speak to them as to spiritual men, but as to carnal. He himself is above in the head, he himself below in his body, that is, the Church; he himself the ladder, who says in John XIV: « I am the way, » etc. To him men ascend, that he may be understood in the heights: and they descend, that he may be nourished in his little members; through him they raise themselves up, that they may look for him on high: and they humble themselves, that they may announce him loftily and with measure.

VERS. 13-15.-- « I am the Lord God of Abraham, » etc. Mystically it signifies the command of the Divinity directed to the humanity of Christ, to whom the land, that is, the Church, is promised; and to his seed, that is, to the Christian people. Whence psalm II: « Ask of me, [0155A] and I will give thee the nations for thy inheritance, and the utmost parts of the earth for thy possession. »

VERS. 16, 17.-- « And when he had awaked, » etc. Whosoever shall have awaked after the torpor of sloth, and shall have truly risen up through the exercise of good work, shall understand in the Church the surpassing grace of God and the entrance of the heavenly kingdom.

VERS. 18.-- « Rising therefore, » etc. [n: (AUG., Q. in Gen., tom. III.)] That Jacob « set up » « the stone which he had put under his head » for a title, and poured oil upon it, he did nothing like to idolatry: because neither then nor afterwards did he frequent the stone by adoring it or by sacrificing to it; but it was a sign and a prophecy pertaining to the anointing, whence Christ is named.

VERS. 19-21.-- « And he called the name of the city [0155B] Bethel., » etc. [n: (HIER., de locis Hebraicis.)] From that which was said, « how terrible is this place, » etc., he imposes on the place the name Bethel, that is, *the house of God,* which before was called Luza, that is, *a nut* or *an almond.* Whence certain men ridiculously think that the Hebrew word *ulam,* is the name of the city: whereas *ulam* is interpreted *before.* This therefore is the order of the reading: He called the name of that place Bethel, and before, Luza was the name of the city. But the ancient writings are full of the word *ulam* or *olam,* which signifies nothing but *before* or *before the porch,* or *a lintel,* or *doorposts,* etc.

VERS. 22.-- « And this stone shall be called the house of God, » etc. It is a prophecy of the house of God that was to be there, and there he himself, returning, sacrificed to God.



## CHAPTER XXIX.

[0155C]

VERS. 1-10.-- « Jacob therefore setting out, » etc. « He saw a well in the field, » etc. The water of the well is the knowledge of the Old Testament: which the stone closed, because the letter of the law hid in it the spiritual sense; but when Christ came the stone was removed, when through the preaching of the New Testament the shadow of the law was shut out and the truth of the Gospel laid open.

*Allegorically.* By the well baptism, by the field the world is expressed: by the three flocks lying beside the well are signified those who pertain to the receiving of the faith of the Trinity, in humility seeking the grace of baptism.

« The stone. » The hardness of unbelief, which being cast away, the baptism of life is received. But the shepherds are the prelates [0155D] of the Church, who, the stone being removed, desire through Jacob to water the flocks: because, when Christ takes away unbelief from the hearts of those whom he has predestined to the sacrament of baptism, they are washed with the wave of purification.

VERS. 11-16.-- « He kissed her. » [n: (AUG.)] It was the custom, chiefly in that simplicity of the ancients, that kinsfolk should kiss kinsfolk, and it is done today in many places. But it may be asked: how did she receive a kiss from one unknown, if Jacob afterwards declared the kinship? It is therefore to be understood: either that he, who knew the kinship, confidently rushed to a kiss, or that Scripture afterwards narrated it by recapitulation, since Jacob first declared who he was.

VERS. 16-19.-- « The name of the elder was Lia, » etc. Victorinus [0156A] the martyr interpreted Rachel and Lia into the likeness of the Church and the Synagogue. He judges that Lia, the elder by birth, signifies the Synagogue, because she first bore the people of God: who is said to be blear-eyed, because the law given through Moses was wrought together and sealed. Rachel, the younger and the fair, first barren, afterwards fruitful, signifies the Church, which is later in time, but holy in body and in spirit. Her eyes are comely, because they deserved to see the Gospel. But she was long barren while the Synagogue brought forth a people. Jacob served for Rachel, and Lia is put under him in her stead: because Christ, that he might take the Church to himself, first joined the Synagogue to himself.

VERS. 20-35.-- « Jacob therefore served seven years for Rachel. » The servitude of Jacob of seven years for two wives signifies the time of the present life, [0156B] which is rolled round in seven days; in which the Lord took the form of a servant, obedient to the Father unto death. Jacob served, because « the Son of man came not to be ministered unto, but to minister [n: (Matth. XX)]: » He fed sheep, and Christ said [n: (Joan. X)]: « I am the good shepherd. » He took to himself for a reward the party-coloured flock: Christ gathered to himself the variety of divers nations. He set three rods, the barks being stripped off, in the troughs of the waters, that by the beholding of them the sheep might be multiplied: and our Lord set before the faithful people, in the water of baptism, the names of the three persons, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost, that he who shall have looked upon this with a full heart may be made a sheep of God.

« And they seemed to him but a few days, » etc. It is to be asked: how was this said, since even a short time [0156C] seems long to them that love? It was said therefore because of the labour of the servitude, which love made easy.

[n: (AUG., lib. XXII contra Faustum.)] Four wives are cast up against Jacob as a huge crime. But since it was then the custom, it was not a crime; now however it is a crime, because it is not the custom, etc., *as far as* save the burning of carnal concupiscence.

[n: (ISID., in Gen., tom. V.)] There are therefore two free wives of Jacob, for both are daughters of the remission of sins, etc., *as far as* and he saw in the beginning the Word with God, and wills to bring forth, and cannot, because « who shall declare his generation? » [n: (Act. VIII.)]

[n: (ISID.)] Two free wives of Jacob, because two lives are preached to us in the body of Jesus Christ, etc., *as far as* But this cannot be in the land of the dying, which [0156D] is signified by these words.

VERS. 26.-- « It is not the custom in our place, » etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] She is called the elder who is the earlier in time, etc., *as far as* to one thing therefore we must tend, but for this many things must be borne.

[n: (ID., ibid.)] Accordingly the life which suits the study of contemplation, etc., *as far as* whence below in XXX: « Rachel was jealous of her sister. »

[n: (ID., ibid.)] Accordingly, because a pure understanding of that divine substance, etc., *as far as* not because a divine power.

## CHAPTER XXX.

VERS. 14-31.-- « And Ruben going out, » etc. [n: (AUG., lib. XXII contra Faustum.)] Why Scripture did not keep silence [0157A] about the thing done, etc., *as far as* and whatsoever is piously and wisely said of this may be wholesomely, or in part, received without the phantasm of carnal thought.

« And Ruben going out, etc. » [n: (AUG.)] *Historically.* But I know that some suppose of this kind, that, taken as food, it brings forth fruitfulness in barren women, and that Rachel therefore insisted that she should receive it, which I do not think, nor would she thus have conceived then. But now, since, after two other births of Lia from that night, the Lord has given her offspring, there is nothing wherefore we should suspect of the mandrake any such thing as we have experienced in no woman. For when I had myself seen these apples, and rejoiced that, because of this place of the sacred reading, they had fallen to me (for it is a rare thing), I searched out their nature as far as I could, [0157B] not by any knowledge further removed from the common sense, which teaches the powers of roots and the virtues of herbs, but so far as sight, smell and taste report, and I found the thing fair, sweet-smelling, but insipid in savour. Why then the woman desired it so greatly I know not, unless perhaps for the rarity of the fruit and the pleasantness of the odour.

VERS. 32-43.-- « Go round all thy flocks, » etc. [n: (HIER., in Quaest. Hebr.)] The sense is much confused among the Seventy, etc., *as far as* the end is near.

[n: (ALC.)] Why Jacob is not accused of fraud in this place, this without doubt he did as a prophet, nor is it to be believed that he did it without spiritual revelation.



## CHAPTER XXXI.

[0157C]

VERS. 1-11.-- « But after he had heard, » etc. . . . . « He changed the wages, » etc. [n: (HIERON. in Quaest. Hebr.)] In place of what we have set down, « he changed my wages ten times, » the Seventy have set « ten years: » whereas the Hebrew word *monim* sounds a number rather than years; and from what follows this sense is proved, that at every bringing forth he changed the condition upon Laban. If he saw a party-coloured beast born, he would say: I will that in time to come party-coloured ones be born to me; then Jacob did not put the rods in the channels, that the young might be born of one colour: which being done, Laban would say that those cattle ought to fall to his own share; thus up to ten times the condition was changed: and whatsoever Laban had proposed should be born to himself, [0157D] was turned into the contrary colour. But now in six years ten times of bringing forth would seem incredible; but read Virgil, in whom it is said: « Twice pregnant are the flocks. » But the nature of Italian sheep and of those of Mesopotamia is said to be the same.

VERS. 12.-- « For I have seen all things, » etc. From this we learn that Jacob did it by no evil guile, but by divine disposition, or because all things were being done in a figure. Whence he deserved to be strengthened by an angelic vision.

VERS. 13-31.-- « Now therefore arise. » [n: (GREG., Moral. XXX, 16.)] After a long servitude the Lord commanded Jacob to return into his own country: then, his father-in-law not knowing it, he hastened away with his wives and his company. But Laban followed after him in mount [0158A] Galaad, seeking the idols with fury, and found them not. But Laban in this place bears the type of the devil. For he is interpreted *a whitening.* But the devil, though he be dark, « transformeth himself into an angel of light. » Him Jacob served, that is, the Jewish people on the part of the reprobate, of whom Christ was born according to the flesh.

VERS. 32-39.--. . . . « He knew not that Rachel had stolen the idols. » [n: (HIERON.)] Where we read « idols, » in the Hebrew *theraphim* is written; which Aquila interprets « figures, » or « images ». But I say this for this cause, that we may know what *theraphim* sounds in the book of Judges.

« Over against mount Galaad, » etc. Not that at that time it was called mount Galaad, but by anticipation [0158B] it is called by that name, by which it was afterwards named.

« Who, taking his brethren, pursued, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] *Historically.* By Laban the world can be expressed: which follows Jacob with fury, because it strives to oppress the elect, who are the members of Christ. Jacob took away the daughter of the world or of the devil, when Christ joined to himself the Church out of the Gentiles, to whom it is said through the prophet in psalm XLIV: « Forget thy people, » etc. In the idols avarice is signified, which is the service of idols. Laban, coming to Jacob, found not the idol, because, the riches of the world being shown to our Redeemer, the devil found no traces of concupiscence. But what Jacob had not, Rachel by sitting covered, who is interpreted *a sheep,* and signifies [0158C] the Church. But to sit is to seek the humility of penance. Whence in psalm CXXVI: « Rise up after you have sat. » Rachel by sitting covers the idols, because the Church, following Christ, has covered over the vice of earthly concupiscence by the humility of penance. Whence in psalm XIII: « Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven, and whose sins are covered. » We therefore Rachel signifies, who by sitting press down the idols, if by doing penance we condemn the faults of avarice; which uncleanness of avarice hinders not those that run manfully, but those that walk effeminately are loosed through the blandishments of the world, which is signified by these words: « It hath befallen me according to the custom of women. » For as it were she made known that she suffered the things of a woman.

[0158D] « That I might kiss my sons and daughters. » After the manner of Scripture he calls the sons and daughters of Jacob his own, which calls kinsmen sons or brethren; whence in what follows: « And Jacob said to his brethren: Bring stones. » In the Gospel also the sons of Mary, the maternal aunt of the Lord, are called his brethren, in Matthew XII.

« I feared lest thou wouldst violently take away thy daughters, but as to thy accusing me of theft, » etc. As if to say, therefore I did not make it known to thee, lest by the bitterness of persecution thou shouldst overcome the weak souls, newly snatched from thee.

VERS. 40-45.-- « Day and night I was parched with heat, and with frost. Sleep fled from mine eyes. » The effect of heat and of cold is one.

VERS. 46-52.-- « Bring stones, » etc. (HIERON. [0159A] *de Loc. Hebr.* ) A heap, in the Hebrew tongue, etc., *as far as* he had changed by the speech in which he dwelt.

[n: (ISID.)] *Mystically.* Among the faithful, both Jews and Gentiles, the witness is a stone standing out into the likeness of Christ; and the heap of stones, which is the multitude of believers.

VERS. 53, 54.-- « Jacob swore, » etc. That is, by the fear wherewith he feared God, whom he commended above, saying, « And the fear, » etc.

VERS. 55.-- « But Laban in the night, etc. » That Laban and Jacob went over the river can signify two peoples coming to the Church through baptism. But Jacob going further forward, Laban returned, because, while the sons of light pass on in the advancement of virtues after baptism, the reprobate, from the dignity they have received, [0159B] return after Satan into apostasy.

## CHAPTER XXXII.

VERS. 2-6.-- « These are the camps of God. » Where *castra* is set, in the Hebrew is *mahanaim* ; that we may know, if at any time the interpreted word is set in another place, what place it signifies. And beautifully, being about to go to his enemy brother, he is received by the choirs of the angels accompanying him.

VERS. 7-9.-- « Jacob feared, » etc. [n: (AUG., Quaest. in Gen. )] It can be asked how he had faith in the promises of God, in that he said this. But it could come to pass that Esau should overthrow his camps, and that then, after that affliction, he should be present, and should fulfil what he promised. And we are admonished by this example that, although we believe in God, [0159C] we should nevertheless do the things that are to be done for the safeguard of our salvation. The words also of Jacob are to be considered, when he says: « God of my father Abraham, » etc., because in these words both human infirmity and the faith of piety appear.

VERS. 10.-- « I passed over the Jordan, » etc. Through baptism the wall from the Gentiles was joined to that which is from the Jews: yet to the primitive Church the fear of the Jews is not lacking, which Esau signifies.

VERS. 11-19.-- « Deliver me from the hand, » etc. So he prays that he may temper hope by fear, and comfort fear by hope, signifying that we should take counsel by reason for our life and for our household, and nevertheless call upon the help of God.

VERS. 20-27.-- « I will appease him, » etc. These things are interposed [0159D] by the writer. For it belongs not to a prudent man to uncover his art, and so to make himself suspected by those with whom he deals.

*[n: (GREG. hom. 14, in Evang., tom. II.)] Mystically.* When Jacob returns to his own parents, etc., *as far as* that with inward sweetness we may lay hold of God.

« And behold a man, » etc. [n: (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.)] In this chiefly the image of the Lord's sacrament is figured, etc., *as far as* that it may no more be able to beget.

VERS. 28, 29.-- « By no means, he says, shall thy name be called Jacob, but, » etc. [n: (HIERON. in Q. Hebr. )] Josephus in the first book of the Antiquities, etc., *as far as* we are led rather by the authority of Scripture, and of the angel, or of God, [0160A] who himself called him Israel, than by that of any secular eloquence.

VERS. 30-32.-- « And Jacob called, » etc. That also which follows, « and he blessed him, and Jacob called the name of that place Phanuel, saying, » etc. In the Hebrew it is called « Phanuel, » that we may know it to be the same place which in the other volumes of Scripture, according to the Hebrews, is read *Phanuel.*

[n: (GREG., lib. IV Moral., cap. 6.)] It is asked: since Truth says: « No man shall see my face; » And: « No man hath seen God at any time, » how does Jacob testify: « I saw the Lord face to face? » [var: Vulg. Gen. 32:30 *Vidi Deum facie ad faciem*, "I have seen God face to face"] For human minds, the inward eye being purged, etc., *as far as* whence I Cor. XIII: « Then shall I know even as I am known. »



## CHAPTER XXXIII.

[0160B]

VERS. 1-9.-- « And Jacob lifting up his eyes. . . . and he divided the children of Lia and of Rachel, » etc. Aquila, *he divided them in half,* that he might make one company of the handmaids with their little ones, and another of the free women with their sons; and might send the handmaids first, the free women second: he himself running out before them all to adore his brother.

VERS. 10-13.-- « For so I have seen, » etc. [n: (AUG., Q. in Gen. tom. III.)] Whether they are the words of a fearful and troubled mind, etc., *as far as* those who are wont to hear and understand Greek.

VERS. 14.-- « And I will follow, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is asked whether he promised his brother a lie. For this, as Scripture relates, he did not do; but he went on by that [0160C] road which led to his own people. Or did he perhaps promise with a truthful mind, but afterwards, on further thought, choose otherwise?

« Socoth. » [n: (HIERON.)] Where we have *tabernacles,* in the Hebrew is read *Socoth* [ed: Migne prints a Hebrew word here, and the full stop that closes the sentence; our transcription of the Latin has lost both.] There is moreover to this day a city beyond the Jordan of this name, in the region of Scythopolis, of which we have written in the book of Places.

VERS. 18-20. « And he passed over into Salem, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] A question arises how the city Sichen is called Salem, since Jerusalem, in which Melchisedech reigned, was formerly called Salem. Either then both cities are of one name, which we find of many places of Judaea, so that the same is the name of a city and of a place in one tribe and another. Or we shall say that this Salem, which [0160D] is now named instead of Sichem, is interpreted *finished* and *perfect:* and that one which was afterwards called Jerusalem, *peaceful.* This word sounds each of the two, the accent being a little turned aside. The Hebrews hand down that the thigh of the limping Jacob was healed there: and that therefore the city obtained the name of *the healed* and *the perfected.*

## CHAPTER XXXIV.

VERS. 1.-- « And Dina went out, » etc. [n: (GREG. in Pastor., c. 30.)] Dina goes out to see the women of a foreign country, when the mind, neglecting its own pursuits and busying itself with the doings of others, strays outside its proper order. Her Sichem, the prince of the land, oppresses, because the devil corrupts her [0161A] whom he has found among outward cares. And his soul was knit to her, because he looks upon her as united to himself through iniquity. And because the mind, coming to itself from its fault, is stricken and strives to weep for what it has committed: the corrupter calls up before its eyes an empty hope and security, that he may take away the profit of its sorrow. Whence it is added: « And flattering he soothed her that was sad. » [var: Vulg. Gen. 34:3 *tristemque delinivit blanditiis*, "and he soothed her, being sad, with flatteries"] For now he speaks of the deeds of others as heavier; now that what has been done is nothing; now that God is merciful, and he promises time for penance: so that, while the mind is led on deceived by these things, penance may be put off, that then it may receive no good things, it whom evils now make sad: and that it may then be the more fully swallowed up in punishments, it which now rejoices in delights.

VERS. 2-32.-- « And when Sichem had seen her. . . . [0161B] and slept with her, » etc. [n: (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.)] And he set his mind upon the soul of Dina the daughter of Jacob, and loved the virgin, and spoke according to the mind of the virgin. How is she called a virgin, if he had already slept with her? unless perhaps "virgin" is a name of age according to the Hebrew manner of speech, or rather, by recapitulation, that is recorded afterwards which was done before. For first he could set his mind upon her soul, and love the virgin, and speak according to the mind of the virgin, and then sleep with her.

« Peaceable. » Where the Seventy have rendered *peaceable,* Aquila has translated *finished, and perfect* for which is read in the Hebrew: *Selemim.* From which it appears that to be true which we said above of Salem.

[0161C] « They consented, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] A little before, Jacob, speaking with Esau, signifies that his sons are infants, etc., *as far as* but his sons are named in this deed as though they were the chief men and the authors of it.

« Having taken up their swords, » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] So ought the pastors of the Church to avenge the sin of the flesh or of the soul with the sword of the spirit, which is the word of God: ready to avenge all disobedience.

« We are few, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] He says this because very many wars could arise, not because he had far fewer than would suffice for the storming of the city.

« Ought they to have abused our sister as a harlot, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] The zeal of the sons of Jacob for the avenging of their sister morally admonishes the pastors of the faithful to have care of the souls committed to them, [0161D] lest they be violently overcome by a bodily offence, or by spiritual fornication. « And let them be ready to avenge all disobedience, » and let them strike the ravisher with the sword of excommunication, lest he escape unpunished.



## CHAPTER XXXV.

VERS. 4-5.-- « They gave him therefore, » etc. [n: (HIER. Q. in Gen., tom. III.)] Plainly it is by this order and by these advances that we must go up into Bethel, that is, into the house of God, which is the Church of Christ. The first thing is to renounce our former errors, and to profess the faith of the one true God, which is to put away strange gods: then to be baptized, which is to be cleansed; and thereafter to walk in newness [0162A] of life, which is to change our garments. And the earrings signify the trappings of false doctrine, glittering in speech, but empty of the sense of truth.

« And the earrings, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is asked: of what sort were the earrings, which were ornaments not pertaining to idolatry? but it must be understood that they were phylacteries of strange gods.

VERS. 6-9.-- « Jacob therefore came to Suzam, » [var: Vulg. Gen. 35:6 *Luzam*] etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is to be noted that three names of this city are recorded, etc., *as far as* for many causes names are added or changed.

[n: (HIERON.)] Behold, it is most manifestly proved that Bethel was formerly called, not Ulam, as was said above, but Luzan, that is, *an almond.*

VERS. 10.-- « Thou shalt no more be called, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] [0162B] It is asked: since it was once said, « Thou shalt no more be called Jacob, » etc., why is he afterwards read to have been called Jacob?
