But this name belongs to that promise in which God is seen as he was not seen before by the fathers, where the old name shall not be: because nothing of oldness shall remain here in the body itself, and the vision of God shall be the highest reward.

[n: (HIERON.)] « Thou shalt no more be called Jacob, » etc. For not yet is the name laid upon him by the angel, but it is foretold that it is to be laid upon him by God. What therefore is promised there as to come, is here taught to be fulfilled.

VERS. 11-13.-- « Increase and multiply. » *Morally.* This promise belongs to the spiritual seed of Jacob, as was already said before of Abraham. [0162C] For all who supplant vices, and put off the old man with his deeds, and bend the eyes of the mind upon God, rightly belong to Jacob, whose faith and deeds they imitate. « And kings shall come forth from his loins, » [var: Vulg. Gen. 35:11 *reges de lumbis tuis egredientur*, "from thy loins"] that is, the saints who according to the will of God truly rule themselves and their subjects: of whose spiritual seed, that is, by the imitation of faith, they are made fruitful in good works.

VERS. 14, 15.-- « But he set up a stone pillar in the place where he had spoken, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] That was done again in this place which had been done before; or that is recorded here which had been done before. But whichever of these it be, Jacob poured his offering upon the stone, not to the stone. Not therefore as idolaters are wont to set altars before a stone, and [0162D] to pour offering to the stone as to God.

VERS. 16, 17.-- « Ephrata. » [n: (HIERON.)] Ephrata and Bethlehem are the name of one city, under a like interpretation. For it is interpreted *fruitful* and *the house of bread,* because of the bread which came down from heaven.

VERS. 18-20.-- « And when she was going forth, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] What does it mean that Rachel, when she was in labour, called the same child Bennoni, *the son of my sorrow?* What but that Paul was to come of that tribe, who should afflict the sons of the Church in the time of his persecution? Otherwise by Benjamin the earthly Jerusalem is figured, which is in the tribe of Benjamin: whose people afflict their mother with grievous sorrow by shedding the blood of the prophets, and by crying out for the death [0163A] of Christ: « His blood be upon us, and upon our children [n: (Matth. XXVII)].

« And it came to pass, when she was giving up her soul. » [var: Vulg. Gen. 35:18 *Egrediente autem anima prae dolore*, "and her soul departing for pain"] [n: (HIERON.)] Since indeed she was dying, « she called his name, the son of my sorrow: but his father called his name Benjamin. » [var: Vulg. Gen. 35:18 *vocavit nomen filii sui Benoni, id est, Filius doloris mei : pater vero appellavit eum Benjamin*] In the Hebrew a likeness of the name sounds, *for son of my sorrow,* which name the dying mother laid upon him, is said *Bennoni. But son of the right hand,* that is, of strength, which Jacob changed it into, is said *Benjamin.* Whence they err who think that Benjamin is interpreted *son of days*: for the right hand is called *iamin,* and ends in *n;* but a day is called *iamim,* and is terminated in *m.*

VERS. 21.-- « Going forth thence, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] The Hebrews will have this place to be, etc., *as far as* or [0163B] what is truer, that by a certain prophecy the mystery to come was even then being shown.

VERS. 22-26.-- « Ruben went, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] *Allegorically.* This crime would not be written, were not the coming perversity of the people declared: although in him there was an outrage, in the Scriptures there is a prophecy of things to come, because by Ruben the firstborn people of Israel is figured: who defiled the couch of the concubine, that is, stained the law of the Old Testament by transgressing. And that the concubine signified the Old Testament, Paul shows, saying, Gal. IV: « Abraham had two sons, one of a bondwoman, and the other of a free woman, » [var: Vulg. Gal. 4:22 *unum de ancilla, et unum de libera*, "one of a bondwoman, and one of a free woman"] etc.

« And there were, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is asked how this is true, etc., *as far as* than that it be taken by synecdoche [0163C].

VERS. 27-29.-- « The city Arbee. » [n: (HIERON.)] For *Arbee* the Seventy have *a plain,* although Hebron is on a mountain. But the same city was anciently called Mambre, from the friend of Abraham.

« The city Arbee, » etc. Of the angels or of the holy souls, to whom he was gathered, no anxiety of temptations remaining, nor peril of sins.

## CHAPTER XXXVI.

VERS. 6-19.-- « He went into another country. » (STRAB) It may be asked: why is Esau said to have gone away into Seir after his father's death, and to have withdrawn from his brother, when already, when Jacob came, he is said to have dwelt in Seir? [0163D] But it could be that, while Jacob was fleeing into Mesopotamia, Esau, from grief at the blessing snatched away, withdrew from his parent, and dwelt in Seir the twenty years in which Jacob was away. Reconciled to him on his return, and having come back again from Seir, he dwelt with his brother so long as their father lived: and when he was dead, he withdrew from his brother, because they were rich, and could not dwell together.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « These are the sons of Seir, » etc. [n: (HIERON. in Q. Hebr. tom. III.)] After he had enumerated the sons of Esau, he goes further back to those who before Esau were chief men in the land of Edom, of the race of the Horrites, who are interpreted *free men.* In Deuteronomy it is written more plainly how the sons of Esau came, and, the Horrites being slain, inherited their land.

[0164A] « The sons of Seir, » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] That is, the *free men* who, as is read in Paralipomenon, first dwelt in mount Seir; and when they were driven out. The Idumeans dwelt in their stead.

VERS. 22, 23.-- « And there was a sister, » etc. [n: (HIER.)] This is Thamna, of whom it was said above: And Thamna was the concubine of Eliphaz, the firstborn of Esau, etc. Therefore he makes mention of the Horrites, because the firstborn of the sons of Esau took a concubine of their daughters. And because Theman is named, and Cenez, and Amalec, and the rest, let us know that afterwards names were laid upon the regions and nations of the Idumeans from these.

VERS. 24-28.-- « This is Ana. » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] Some say simply that he found hot waters in the desert, which we call thermae.

[0164B] « This is Ana, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Various things are disputed among the Hebrews concerning this chapter, etc., *as far as* that mules might be born against nature.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « These are the dukes. » He speaks of those going before, who are of Seir; not of these whom he begins to number, who are of Esau.

VERS. 31-43.-- « And the kings. » It is to be known that he does not record all the kings who were before the children of Ismael had kings, of whom the first was Saul, but only all those who reigned before Moses died. « And the kings. » He records according to the time in which the writer was living, but when Seir, who begot these men, dwelt there, Esau not yet having come into that land, it was called from him the land of Edom.

[n: (AUG., Q. in Gen., t. III.)] It is no wonder that to those numbering [0164C] from Abraham through Esau the father of the nation of Edom, etc., *as far as* for in that order where more are numbered, they died sooner than here where fewer are.

« Husan. » [n: (HIER.)] Whom some suspect to be Job, as is added at the end of his own volume; but the Hebrews affirm that he was begotten of the stock of Nachor, as has already been said above.



## CHAPTER XXXVII.

VERS. 2.-- « Joseph, when he was sixteen years old, » etc., [n: (AUG. in Q. super Genesin. )] *Historically.* How can the death of Isaac, etc., *as far as* yet in Isaac's lifetime Joseph began to be seventeen years old.

« And he was with the sons of Bala, » etc. Joseph, one of [0164D] the twelve sons of Jacob, beloved of his father beyond the rest, signified Christ, whom, born according to the flesh, God the Father preferred to the rest of the brethren begotten of the stock of Abraham. Whence Jacob loved him, « because he had begotten him in his old age. » For when the world was growing old, the Son of God was born of the Virgin, as it were a son of old age.

« And he was with, » etc. In the primitive Church he did not cast off those who clave to him through the preaching of false apostles, whom the handmaids of Jacob signify, whom also he would not forbid, when they wrought miracles in his name.

VERS. 3.-- « And he made him a coat of divers colours. » [n: (HIER.)] For *a parti-coloured tunic* Aquila has rendered [0165A] ἀστραγάλον, that is, *reaching to the ankles,* Symmachus *sleeved,* either because it came down as far as the ankles and was marked out with a wonderful variety by the craftsman's hands, or because it had sleeves: for the ancients used rather colobia, that is, garments without sleeves.

[n: (GREG., hom. XXV in Evang.)] Because Joseph is described as having persevered just among his brethren to the end of his life, he alone is said to have had a coat reaching to the ankles. What is a coat reaching to the ankles, but a completed action? For as it were a coat stretched down covered the ankle of the body, when a good action rules us before the eyes of God to the bounds of life. Whence Moses commanded the tail of the victim to be offered, that we may complete with a persevering end the good work which we begin.

VERS. 4-9.-- « And his brethren seeing, » [0165B] etc. The brethren of Christ according to the flesh are the Jews, of whose kindred he took flesh. But because they saw that the grace of virtues was wrought in him, which bore witness to the Father's especial love, they envied him, and could not speak anything peaceably to him, and again and again attempted snares, and blasphemously said that the Master of the household cast out demons by Beelzebub the prince of demons.

[n: (ISID.)] This was spiritually fulfilled in Christ, etc., *as far as* in the figure of the stars.

VERS. 10, 11.-- « Shall I, » etc. It may be asked according to the letter when he was adored by his father, who is designated by the sun; and by his mother, who is designated by the moon. For neither is Jacob read to have adored him when he went down into Egypt: and his mother was already dead: [0165C] but let us have recourse to the allegory, which is fulfilled in Christ.

VERS. 12-22.-- « And when his brethren, » etc., « in feeding the flocks, » *as far as* « Bring me word, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] Jacob sends his son to take care of his brethren; and God the Father his only-begotten Son, to visit the human race sick with sins. Whence Galatians, chapter four: « God sent his Son in the likeness of sinful flesh. » [var: Vulg. Rom. 8:3 *Deus Filium suum mittens in similitudinem carnis peccati*; the chapter cited, Gal. 4:4, has *Misit Deus Filium suum, factum ex muliere*] Joseph was sent to see whether all things were prosperous with the sheep: and Christ says, in Matthew the fifteenth: « I came not but to the sheep that had perished of the house of Israel. » [var: Vulg. Matt. 15:24 *Non sum missus nisi ad oves, quae perierunt domus Israel*, "I was not sent but to the sheep that are perished"]

« In Dothaim. » *A falling away.* For they were in a great falling away, who were thinking of fratricide. « Ruben. » Foreseeing this, the prophetic word, which [0165D] Ruben signifies (who is interpreted *the vision of the son* ), deterred them by prophetic threatenings, that they might keep themselves innocent of the blood of Christ, their brother according to the flesh.

VERS. 23, 24.-- « They stripped him, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] *Mystically.* The Jews too stripped Christ by death of his bodily coat, and that of divers colours, that is, adorned with the variety of all the virtues. And they sprinkled the coat with the blood of a kid: because, accusing him by false testimonies, they brought him into the odium of sin. Joseph is sent into the cistern, that is, into the pit: and Christ, stripped of human flesh, went down to hell.

VERS. 25-27.-- « They saw travellers, Ismaelites. » [n: (AUG.)] It is asked why Scripture [names them] Ismaelites, etc., [0166A] *as far as* who say that Agar and Cetura were one and the same?

VERS. 28-33.-- « Drawing him out. » [n: (ISID.)] *Allegorically.* Joseph, lifted out of the cistern, is sold to Ismaelites that had been sold: and Christ, returned from hell, is bought by all nations in the traffic of faith. The one is sold off for thirty pieces of silver by the counsel of Judas, and this one is sold for the same number by the counsel of Judas Iscariot.

« They sold. » [n: (HIERON.)] The Hebrew verity has « pieces of silver. » for neither ought the Lord to have been sold for a baser metal than Joseph.

VERS. 34.-- « And having rent his garments, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] Jacob, bewailing the losses of his posterity, mourned as a father for a son that was lost, and as a prophet for the destruction of the Jews [n: (Matth. XXVI)]. He rent his garment: [0166B] which in the Lord's passion the chief of the priests did [n: (Luc. XXIII)]. But the veil of the temple also was rent, that he might prophesy his people stripped bare, and show the kingdom divided.

VERS. 35, 36.-- « I will go down to my son. » etc. It is asked: is this to be understood of the wicked only, or do the good also go down into hell? If the wicked only, how does this man say that he wishes to go down mourning to his son? For it is not credible that he is in the pains of hell. Or are they the words of a troubled and grieving man, magnifying his own evils here also? or is it credible that near hell there were certain hidden places, and less penal, before the Lord's passion, to which the saints also went down? because none could enter paradise, until Christ should take away the flaming [0166C] sword.

[n: (HIERON.)] But the Madianites sold Joseph in Egypt to Putiphar the eunuch, Pharaoh's chief cook. For the most part Scripture puts ἀρχιμαγείρους, that is, chiefs of the cooks, for masters of an army; for μάγειρος in Greek is *a cook,* and there is said μαγειρειύεν, which is *to kill:* because cooks kill the beasts that are to be cooked. Joseph therefore was sold to the chief of the butchers, not to Petefre, as it is written in the Latin, but to Putiphar the eunuch. Whereupon it is asked: how is he afterwards said to have a wife? The Hebrews hand down that Joseph was bought by this man for a foul service because of his exceeding beauty, and that afterwards, his manhood being withered by his master, he was chosen according to the custom of the hierophants, that is, of those who speak sacred things, into the pontificate of Heliopolis: [0166D] and that Aseneth was this man's daughter, whom Joseph afterwards took to wife.



## CHAPTER XXXVIII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « At the same time, » etc. [n: (AUG., Q. in Gen.)] It is asked: how could these things have come to pass? For if, after Joseph came into Egypt, etc., *as far as* that he fell into love of her whom he took to wife, Joseph not yet being sold.

« Judas, » etc. He, like the other patriarchs, signifies Christ, who went down from the baptism [sic: *ab baptismum*] of John. For Hiras is interpreted *seeing God:* Odollamite, *testimony in water,* because John, seeing Christ coming to the baptism, said: « Behold [0167A] the Lamb of God, » etc. [n: (Joan. I.)] To this testimony the Synagogue clave to Christ, which Sue, the daughter of the Chanaanite, signifies, because to her was to be given the land *of humble profession,* which is the interpretation of Chanaan. This Synagogue therefore first clave to Christ, when Peter and Andrew, and the rest of John's disciples, were made disciples of Christ. In what order these things were fulfilled the history following signifies, relating how Judas went in to Thamar, who signifies the same as Sue.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « And she conceived, » etc. The Synagogue bore princes, who were set over her as husbands: of whom some were harmful, whom Her signifies, who is interpreted *made of skin* (for a skin signifies death), because the first parents, clothed with skins, were made over to death; others useless, whom Onan signifies, [0167B] who is interpreted *their sorrow,* namely to those whom they profit not.

VERS. 5-12.-- « She bore a third also, » etc. [n: (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.)] The third son, who is not joined to Thamar, etc., *as far as* and therefore as an Odollamite he bore testimony in water.

« Judas, » etc. From Juda came forth the royal tribe, which long ruled the Jewish people, which Thamar signifies, who is interpreted *bitterness,* because she showed herself bitter to Christ in his passion.

[n: (ISID.)] The Jewish people, to whom kings from the tribe of Juda, etc., *as far as* yet God slew them both, because he takes away the kingdom from such men.

VERS. 13.-- « To shear the sheep. » [n: (STRAB.)] *Allegorically.* The Lord came to shear the sheep, that is, [0167C] to unburden them of their sins, of which it is said: « Thy teeth as flocks of the shorn. » Thamar changes her habit: for she is also interpreted *changing*; she changes both habit and name, that of the Synagogue there may be a Church. But the name of bitterness remains: not that in which she ministered gall to the Lord, but that in which Peter wept bitterly. For Judas too is called *Confession*, let bitterness therefore be mingled with Confession, that true penance may be prefigured. This penance is made fruitful, the Church being established among all nations. For it behoved Christ to suffer, and to rise again, and that penance and remission of sins should be preached in his name among all nations. The habit of a harlot too is the confession of sins: for Thamar signifies the Church called out of the Gentiles; sitting in this habit at the gate of Enaiim [0167D], which is interpreted *a fountain:* because she was running like a hart to the fountains of waters, to come to the seed of Abraham. She is made fruitful, he not knowing it, according to that of Psalm XVII. « The people which I knew not hath served me. » She received in secret the ring, the necklace, and the staff, because she is sealed by vocation, adorned by justification, exalted by glorification. « And whom he predestinated, them he also called; and whom he called, them he also justified [n: (Rom. VIII)]; » but in secret, where the conception of holy fruitfulness is made.

VERS. 14-19.-- « Who, having laid aside the garments of her widowhood, » etc. It is to be noted also that in the times of the patriarchs there were fixed garments of widows, and not such as those of married women.

[0168A] « She sat in the crossway. » [n: (HIERON.)] « And she sat at the gate of Enaiim, which is in the way to Thamna. » The Hebrew word Enaiim is rendered *eyes.* It is not therefore the name of a place, and the sense is, « She sat in the crossway, » or at the meeting of the roads, where a traveller ought to look the more carefully where he is going.

VERS. 20-24.-- « And Judas sent. » The promised kid is sent as to a harlot, that is, the reproach of sin, by the Odollamite, as by one rebuking, « brood of vipers, » etc. But the reproach of sin did not find her whom the bitterness of confession had changed. In this deed of Juda it is not the sincerity of justice that is discerned, but the faithfulness of his promise. For if he had kept justice by keeping chastity, he would not have gone in, as he supposed, to a harlot; but what he promised, [0168B] he faithfully sent by the shepherd.

VERS. 25.-- « And when she was led forth, etc. » [n: (ISID.)] But afterwards, by the public tokens of the ring, the necklace and the staff, she overcame the Jews who judged rashly, whom Judas already signified; who to this day also say that this is not the people of Christ, nor the seed of Abraham, but, when the proofs of our vocation, justification and glorification are brought forward, they are confounded, and will confess that we rather than they have been justified. For the Church, which is accused by the Jews as an adulteress against the law, declares that she has the pledges: but she shows the staff, the sign of the passion, and the necklace, the lawful things of the law, and the ring, the pledge of immortality.

VERS. 26.--Who, when they were acknowledged, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] « And Judas acknowledged, etc. In the Hebrew it is found: « she is justified from me; » not that she was just, but that [0168C] in comparison with him she had done less ill, not in wandering baseness, but in seeking children.

VERS. 27, 28.-- « And when she was in labour, » etc. That the first, who is called Zara, put forth his hand, and the midwife bound the scarlet, and, he himself drawing his hand back, the later, who is called Phares, stretched out his hand, and in being born went before him, signified that Israel would stretch out his hand in the work of the law, and would draw it back defiled with the blood of the prophets and of Christ himself: but that the people of the Gentiles would afterwards break forth, so that « the first should be last, and the last first. »

VERS. 29, 30.-- « Why is the wall divided, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] For *a wall* Aquila and Symmachus have translated *a division,* which in Hebrew is said *Phares.* From this therefore, that he divided the membrane of the afterbirth, he took the name *of division* [0168D]. Whence also the Pharisees, who had separated themselves from the people as though they were just, were called Pharisees, that is, *divided.*

Zara is interpreted *the rising.* Because he appeared first, or because very many just men were born of him, as is said in Paralipomenon.

## CHAPTER XXXIX.

VERS. 1, 6.-- « Joseph therefore was brought into Egypt, » etc. [n: (AUG. in Gen.)] Scripture returns to the order from which it had departed, that it may relate those things which were done above. « And he was, » etc. [n: (GREG., lib. VI Moral., c. 11 seq.)] Many, puffed up with human wisdom, while they look upon the divine judgments as contrary to their own desires, etc., *as far as* and lightens his burdens, which he bears willingly.

[0169A]VERS. 7-11.-- « And after many days his mistress cast, » etc. [n: (GREG. lib. XXX Moral., cap. 19.)] He who is lifted up by the successes of the world, etc., *as far as* for because pleasure is born of prosperity, let it be struck down by the consideration of the same.

« His mistress cast her eyes, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] This is a figure of the Synagogue, etc., *as far as* for whence he had gone down as though to be punished, thence he set others free.

VERS. 12-23.-- « Who, leaving the cloak in her hand, » etc. So do the allurements of the flesh tempt us, and shake the garment of flesh: but the chaste man had rather break every bond than subject himself to baseness.

Who leaving, » etc. [n: (GREG.)] *Allegorically.* Because, while the Synagogue, believing Christ a mere man, wished to bind him in an adulterous embrace, he cast the covering [0169B] of the letter before her eyes, and offered himself to be seen by the Gentiles, that the power of his godhead might be known. Whence to this day, while Moses is read, a veil is upon the eyes of their heart, because the adulteress kept the cloak, and lost naked him whom she was wrongfully holding.



## CHAPTER XL.

VERS. 1-8.-- « These things being thus done, it happened that two eunuchs sinned, » etc. [n: (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.)] Joseph found two eunuchs in the prison, etc., *as far as* he is hanged upon the wood of transgression [sic: *trangressionis*].

« The butler of the king of Egypt, and the baker, against their lord, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Where we have put « the chief of the vintners » in the Hebrew is found *masqueh* [ed: Migne prints a Hebrew word here; our transcription of the Latin has lost it, leaving the comma that follows standing free.], that [0169C] word which we read long since in the name of Abraham's servant, whom we may in the common way call *a butler,* and to this day among the barbarians it is of the greatest dignity to have handed the cup to the king.

VERS. 9-15.-- « I saw before me a vine, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] « And behold a vine in my sight, and on the vine three stems, and it budding forth three stems. » The Hebrew tongue signifies three shoots, three branches, or layers, which are called by them *Sarigim* [ed: Migne prints a Hebrew word here; our transcription of the Latin has lost it, leaving the full stop that follows standing free.].

VERS. 16-19.-- « Three baskets. » [n: (AUG. Quaest. in Gen.)] Where in the Latin copies is written « three baskets of meal, » etc., *as far as* and of every kind of baker's work, namely in the same uppermost basket.

VERS. 20-23.-- « The birthday of Pharaoh. » Pharaoh [0169D] and Herod kept the day of their birth, because they thought that from the hour of their birth they had been kings.

## CHAPTER XLI.

VERS. 1.-- « After two years Pharaoh saw a dream. He thought that he stood, » etc. [n: (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.)] As the servant of Abraham said, etc., *as far as* where earthly animals dwell.

VERS. 2-8.-- « Out of which came up seven oxen, » etc. [n: (HIERON. Q. Hebr.)] Behold, out of the river came up seven kine fair to look upon, etc., *as far as* they joined the Greek χ.

VERS. 9-16.-- « Then at length the chief of the butlers remembering, » etc. Because the Gentile people at last [0170A] by the setting forth of things past brought itself into the hope of things to come.

VERS. 17-32.-- « I thought that I stood, » etc. The seven years, which are shown by the seven full ears, or by the seven fat kine, signify the spiritual gifts by which the plenty of faith overflows with a bountiful loving-kindness. But the seven barren and lean ones, the famine of truth and of justice in the last time.

VERS. 33.-- « Now therefore, » etc. We see this fulfilled in the time of grace: because, apostolic doctrine abounding and being ministered by the sevenfold Spirit, bishops and teachers were ordained by divine disposition through the several cities, who out of the abundance of doctrine divinely bestowed upon them wrote the holy books, wherewith, when famine of the word abounds and teachers fail, we may relieve our need [0170B].

VERS. 34-37.-- « And the fifth part of the fruits, » etc. It is to be noted that the Egyptians gather the fifth part of the corn into the granaries of the king: because the sons of darkness, given over to the five senses, care not for heavenly things: but the true Israelites give tithes, because they labour to restore the heavenly country, of which a tenth part fell.

[n: (ISID.)] Joseph gathered through seven years all the abundance of the corn: that is, storing the grain of faith in the granaries of the saints, through the seven gifts, as it were through seven years, so that when the seven years of want shall have begun, that is, when the iniquity of the seven capital crimes shall have come under Antichrist, when there shall be a famine of faith and of salvation, then the saints may have a plentiful [0170C] harvest of justice, lest their faith fail through want of the word.

VERS. 38-40.-- « Who is full of the Spirit of God, » etc. [n: (AUG. Q. in Gen.)] For the third time, unless I am mistaken, the Holy Spirit is intimated to us in this book. First where it was said above [n: (cap. I)]: « The Spirit of God moved over the waters, » secondly where God says above [n: (cap. VI)]: « My Spirit shall not remain in these men, » [var: Vulg. Gen. 6:3 *Non permanebit spiritus meus in homine in aeternum, quia caro est*, "in man for ever, for he is flesh"] etc. Thirdly when Pharaoh says that the Spirit of God is in Joseph: yet we do not yet read of the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 41.-- « And Pharaoh said again, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] *Allegorically.* Joseph, who bore the type of Christ, earned a chariot, and a herald made proclamation before him, and Pharaoh set him over the whole land of Egypt: and our Lord, after he was sold away [0170D] by Juda, as Joseph by his brethren, and rose from the prison of hell, ascended the chariot of the heavenly kingdom, of which it is said in psalm LXVII: « The chariot of God is manifold by ten thousands; » and he received from the Father the power of preaching and of judging. Whence Phil. II: « He gave him a name which is above every name, » [var: Vulg. Phil. 2:9 *donavit illi nomen, quod est super omne nomen*, "he bestowed on him a name"] etc. He received also a ring, that is, the pontificate of faith, wherewith the souls of believers are sealed with the sign of salvation, and upon our foreheads and hearts the image of the eternal king is stamped by the sign of the cross. He is clothed with a robe of fine linen, that is, with holy flesh more shining than fine linen, and with the robe of immortality. He received also a golden collar, that is, a good understanding. A herald goes before him, that is, John the Baptist, who says in Matth. III: « Prepare the way [0171A] of the Lord. » He shall have another herald also, because he shall come at the trumpet of the angel.

VERS. 41, 42.-- « Behold, I have set thee over the whole land of Egypt, » etc. Joseph, brought out of the prison, is set over Egypt; and Christ rising again received « a name which is above every name, that in the name of Jesus every knee should bow [n: (Philipp. II)]. »

VERS. 43, 44.-- « A herald proclaiming, » etc. [n: (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr. )] « and a herald cried before him, and he set him over all the land of Egypt. » For which Aquila has translated: « And he cried in his sight, To the bending of the knee. » Symmachus, etc., *as far as* that in respect of the prudence of all he was a father, but in respect of age a most tender youth [0171B] and a boy.

VERS. 45-49.-- « And he called, » etc. [n: (HIER., ibid. )] And Pharaoh called the name of Joseph, etc., *as far as* that that suspicion of the Hebrews concerning him, of which we spoke before, may be proved true.

« And he gave him a wife, » etc. [n: (AUG., ubi supra. )] It is wont to be asked of which Putiphar, otherwise Petefre, as very many have it, and corruptly, as was said above, etc., *as far as* it is not perilous to faith, nor contrary to the truth of the Scriptures.

VERS. 50-58.-- « And there were born to Joseph two sons, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] Observe, because of the question which is to be put afterwards concerning the sons of Joseph, that before the time of the famine, in which Jacob entered into Egypt, Joseph had only two sons, Manasses and [0171C] Ephraim.

VERS. 56, 57.-- « And Joseph opened all the granaries, » etc. *Allegorically.* Joseph saves Egypt from the want of corn, and Christ frees the world from the famine of the word. Christ opened his granaries into the whole earth, and by the dispensing of his corn subdued all things. Had the brethren not sold Joseph, Egypt had failed: had the Jews not crucified Christ, the world had perished. Joseph is interpreted *increase* or *enlargement:* but in that Joseph none had the enlargement save Egypt alone; in ours, the whole world. He dispensed wheat; ours the word of God. « Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth [n: (Psal. XVIII)]. »



## CHAPTER XLII.

[0171D]

VERS. 1-15.-- « And Jacob hearing that food was sold in Egypt, » etc. [n: (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.)] There is corn in Egypt: God the Father says also, Osee XI: « Out of Egypt I have called my Son. » Ten of the elder brethren go down, that is, the Jews, set under the decalogue; whom he himself knowing, was not acknowledged by them; the Hebrews are known by Christ, and they themselves do not acknowledge him; they gave him money; but Joseph, that is, Christ, gave wheat, and gave back the silver, because it is not with money that Christ is bought, but with grace.

VERS. 16-22.-- « Send one of you . . . . . otherwise, by the health of Pharaoh, you are spies. » [0172A] [n: (AUG. Quaest. in Gen. )] What is it that Joseph, a wise man, etc., *as far as* he shall be God, for he shall not be, but shall be accounted

VERS. 22-26.-- « And they knew not, » etc. [n: (Aug., ibid. )] What is it that, when they were speaking among themselves in penitence of Joseph, etc., *as far as* as they had sold him into foreign provinces.

VERS. 27-34. « And one having opened, » etc. The brethren of Joseph give silver, because the Jews when converted let go the letter that killeth, and receive the spirit that quickeneth: yet the silver is found in the mouth of the sacks, because for the sake of the fruit of spiritual understanding the beauty of eloquence is not forsaken.

VERS. 35, 36.-- « Each of them found, » etc. The brethren of Joseph gave money in the buying of corn, [0172B] but they received it back when they had received the corn, because our Joseph seeks not our goods, but us. For he gives his gifts freely, and in the buying of what is ours makes us richer.

VERS. 37.-- « To whom Ruben answered, » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] Ruben says: « Kill my two sons, if I bring him not back to thee, » etc., and yet he did not prevail. But Judas, saying: « I shall be guilty of sin against my father for ever, » [var: Vulg. Gen. 43:9 *ero peccati reus in te omni tempore*, "guilty of sin against thee"] obtained it, because Benjamin was committed to him. It is more, therefore, to be guilty of sin than to be slain in the body.

VERS. 38.-- « You shall bring down my grey hairs, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is asked: does he speak thus of going down to hell because it is with sorrow, or, even if sorrow were absent, as one about to go down to hell by dying? For concerning hell [0172C] there is a great question, and what Scripture holds of it, wherever this is mentioned, must be observed.

## CHAPTER XLIII.

VERS. 11-22.-- « A little rosin and honey and storax, » etc. [n: (HIER. Quaest. Hebr., tom. III.)] Somewhat of rosin, and of honey, etc., *as far as* because in him are laid up divers spices.

VERS. 23-28.-- « Your God and the God of your father hath given you treasures, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] It seems a lie, but it must be believed to signify something. For the silver, which is given and is not diminished, which also has been called tried, is perhaps that of which it is said in psalm XI: « The words of the Lord are chaste words, silver tried by the fire, proved of the earth, purified [0172D] seven times, » that is, tried perfectly.

VERS. 29-31.-- « And Joseph lifting up his eyes saw Benjamin, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] In Acts IX a light as it were shone round about Paul, who is the little one, because he did not yet bear the ripe age of faith. Whence he is also read of as a young man, while he kept the garments of those who stoned Stephen. Joseph wept; the blindness of Paul is the weeping of Christ; Christ washed his face, when Paul is baptized, through whom Christ is to be seen by very many.

[n: (AUG.)] The affection of a good mind is always prone to piety. Whence in Joseph we may consider an abundance of virtues. He was chaste, when he spurned the shamelessness of his mistress; wise, when with diligent search he explored the minds of his brethren, how they felt [0173A] toward their brother by the same mother; he was just in applying discipline to offenders; loving, in the turning back of the penitent.

VERS. 32, 33.-- « And when these were set on, » etc. The dishes were set apart for Joseph as for a stranger, and apart for his brethren, as for men foreign by nation: and for the Egyptians also apart, as for natives, who thought a banquet with foreigners to be profane.

VERS. 34.-- « And they drank and were made drunk, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] Drunkards are wont to bring forward this testimony in their own defence, because of Joseph, who is commended as very wise. But this word is put in the Scriptures for fulness, whence in psalm LXIV: « Thou hast visited the earth and hast made it drunk, » because this is set down in praise of blessing, and the gift of God is recorded; it appears [0173B] that this drunkenness signifies fulness: for to be made drunk as drunkards are made drunk is not useful even to the earth itself; since by more moisture than suffices it is corrupted; like the life of a drunkard, who fills himself not with satiety, but drowns himself in a flood.

## CHAP. XLIV.

VERS. 5-14.-- « The cup which you have stolen, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] What does it mean that Joseph's cup was found in Benjamin's sack, but that in Paul's heart the eloquence of heavenly doctrine already shone forth, while he was learned in the law? But because he was not subject to God, the cup was within the sack, doctrine within the law, the lamp within the bushel. Yet Ananias, being sent, laid his hand upon him, loosed the purse, and the silver [0173C] shone out, and, the scales falling away as it were fetters, the sack being loosed, that is, the veil of the law laid aside, he obtained the liberty of grace, and with unveiled face of speech preaches the Gospel.

VERS. 15.-- « Know you not that there is none like me in the science of divining? » [n: (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.)] What this may mean is wont to be asked. Whether because it was said not in earnest but in jest, etc., *as far as* the brother being recognized, whom they thought lost to them.

VERS. 16-34.-- « To whom Judas, » etc. [n: (AUG., ubi supra. )] Many things in the narration of Judas are said otherwise than as Joseph had dealt with them, etc., *as far as* but he who knew that he knew it was inserting into the narration, that he might bend him to mercy.



## CHAPTER XLV.

[0173D]

VERS. 7-9.-- « God sent me, » etc. [n: (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.)] What is it that according to other copies is read thus: « For God sent me before you, that your remnant should remain upon the earth, and that he might nourish up a great seed of your remnant? » For this does not agree in every point, etc., *as far as* « and so all Israel should be saved. »

VERS. 10-15.-- « Thou shalt dwell in the land of Gessen, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] In the Hebrew there stands *Gosen* ( )[ed: Migne prints a Hebrew word within these parentheses; our digitized source has lost it and left them empty.]. Whence the error grew up that the land of Gessen is in Arabia. Further, as it stands in our codices, it is written with a final *m*, Gessem, which by no means pleases me, as signifying a land rained upon. For Gessem is turned into *rain* [0174A], where also in very many copies Gessem is found.

VERS. 16-21.-- « And Pharao was glad, » etc. These words of Pharao, which he spoke to Joseph concerning Jacob and his sons, signify the joys of the Gentile people over the conversion of the Jews. For great is the devotion of the Church of the Gentiles that the people of Israel should come to the faith, to whom, if they be converted and become partakers of the faith, he promises the spiritual riches of the virtues, and the grace of the Holy Spirit, which he that receives shall not labour under want of good.

VERS. 22-28.-- « But to Benjamin he gave, » etc. Three hundred pieces of silver are given to Benjamin, which he received from Christ who preaches the Trinity, or the cross of Christ. Whence Paul says in the first to the Corinthians, II: « I judged not myself [0174B] to know any thing among you, but Christ Jesus, and him crucified. » He received five robes, that is, the wisdom of all the senses. Paul therefore excels in the abundance of his merits: but his brethren also, that is, the other preachers, have their own grace, that is, two robes apiece, that they may confess Christ to be God and man. Whence Prov. XXXI: « All her household are clothed with double garments, » that is, with mystical and moral understanding. Gifts too are sent to the father, and Christ invites his people with gifts promised. Asses bear the gifts, that is, the Gentiles, useless and toilsome: but now, useful, they bear gifts in the type of Christ, in the Gospel the bestower of gifts.

[n: (ISID.)] Jacob was afraid, that is, the unbelieving people: but after he came to know the deeds of Christ, his spirit [0174C] revived, and he who seemed dead is quickened by faith in the resurrection of Christ. The people of the Jews therefore is called by his sons, that is, by Peter, Paul and John, unto grace. Judas meets him, who is interpreted *confession,* because confession goes before those whom faithlessness possessed before: and so Joseph, that is, Christ, meets him, to take up at the last time the people of the Jews, now aged in years, not according to merits, but according to the election of grace, and to lay his hand upon his eyes, and take away his blindness; which he deferred, that he should believe last who before did not think that it was to be believed. Whence to the Romans in the eleventh chapter: « Because blindness in part is made in Israel, until the fulness of the Gentiles should come in, and so all Israel should be saved. »[var: Vulg. Rom 11:25 *quia caecitas ex parte contigit in Israel*, "because blindness in part hath befallen Israel."]

## CHAPTER XLVI.

[0174D]

VERS. 26.-- « All the souls that went in with Jacob into Egypt, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] It must be considered what answer is to be made to those who by this testimony maintain that souls are propagated from parents together with bodies: for that "souls" is said for "men," by a figurative manner of speech naming the whole from a part, no one doubts. But how we are to alienate that very part, that is, the soul by whose name the whole man is signified, from that which is said, « they came forth out of his thighs, »[var: Vulg. Gn 46:26 *egressae sunt de femore illius*, "went out of his thigh."] so as to take it that flesh only was born of him (although souls alone are named), the modes of speech in the Scriptures must be sought out.

VERS. 27.-- « And the sons of Joseph, » etc. *(HIERON. [0175A] Quaest. Hebr., tom. III.)* All the souls of the house of Jacob, etc., *as far as* although very many hand down that Luke, as a proselyte, was ignorant of Hebrew letters.

VERS. 28-31.-- « And he sent Judas, » etc. [n: (HIER., ibid. )] But Judas he sent before him to Joseph, etc., *as far as* they suppose that the Arsenoite district was so called of old.

VERS. 32-34.-- « And they are men that are shepherds, » etc. [n: (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.)] It is commended in the patriarchs that they were feeders of cattle from their childhood, etc., *as far as* at the end they shall have a most ordered and everlasting happiness.



## CHAPTER XLVII.

VERS. 9, 10.-- « The days of my pilgrimage, » etc. [n: (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.)] What is it that Jacob says [0175B] to Pharao, etc., *as far as* they do not abide in the house for ever.

VERS. 11.-- « But Joseph to his father and his brethren . . . Ramesse. » It must be asked whether the land of Ramesses is itself Gessen: for this they had sought, and Pharao had commanded that it be given.

[n: (ISID.)] *Allegorically.* Joseph handed over to his father and his brethren the best land, Gessen, providing them with victuals, because famine had oppressed the land: so also the Lord, to his kindred, that is, to the patriarchs and prophets, of whom he is according to the flesh, or to all the saints, of whom he says in the Gospel, Matth. II: « These are my brethren, who do the will of my Father, »[var: Vulg. Mt 12:50 *Quicumque enim fecerit voluntatem Patris mei, qui in caelis est, ipse meus frater, et soror, et mater est*, "Whosoever shall do the will of my Father, who is in heaven, he is my brother, and sister, and mother."] to these he gives the best land, of which it is said, psal. XXVI: « I believe to see the good things of the Lord in the land of the living. »

[0175C]VERS. 12-15.-- « Providing victuals, to every one, » etc. Joseph measured out wheat to his own father, and yet his father did not adore him, neither when he saw him nor when he received the wheat: how then do we think the dream fulfilled, unless it contain the prophecy of a greater thing.

VERS. 16-19.-- « Bring your cattle, and I will give you, » etc. It is asked, since Joseph was gathering the corn whereby men might live, whence the cattle were living, when so great a famine had grown strong, and the brethren of Joseph said to Pharao: « There is no pasture for the cattle of thy servants, »[var: Vulg. Gn 47:4 *non est herba gregibus servorum tuorum*, "there is no grass for the flocks of thy servants."] etc. If therefore in that famine the pastures had failed in the land of Chanaan, why had they not failed in Egypt, since the same famine was everywhere? Or could it be, as is reported by those who know the places, that in the many marshes of Egypt pastures might well not be wanting, even [0175D] when there was a famine of the corn which is wont to come forth by the inundation of the Nile: those marshes are said to bring forth pasture more fruitfully when the water of the Nile rises less.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « He bought therefore, » etc. Joseph is not accused of injustice or of iniquity, since his fidelity is even commended from this, that he accepted the favour of no person, but weighed out with an even balance according to the buyer's price.

But mystically it signifies that all who are in the spiritual Egypt and court her riches are slaves. For no Egyptian is free, who is carnal, and sold under sin: for Pharao made them subject; whence perhaps it is said, Exod. XX: « I am the Lord thy God, [0176A] who brought thee out of Egypt, out of the house of bondage. »[var: Vulg. Ex 20:2 *qui eduxi te de terra Aegypti*, "who brought thee out of the land of Egypt."] Whence Rom. VI: « Let not sin reign in your mortal body. » etc. And in the same place: « You are the servants of him whom you have obeyed, whether of sin unto death, or of obedience unto justice. »[var: Vulg. Rom 6:16 *servi estis ejus, cui obeditis, sive peccati ad mortem*, "you are the servants of him whom you obey, whether of sin unto death."] But the Egyptians were brought by violence into slavery, the Hebrews indeed by violence into service. The Egyptians, however, sold their own selves, and it is no fault of the steward where what is deserved is repaid according to merits. This Paul also did, when he delivers to Satan that man who was unworthy of the number or the fellowship of the saints [n: (I Cor. V)]. Let no one therefore say that Paul acted too harshly, who cast the man out of the Church, that being expelled he might learn not to blaspheme: for the will of man is iniquitous, when, loving earthly things, it despises the heavenly, and for covetousness subjects itself to the devil. But God's [0176B] permission is just, when he leaves to his own choice, that he may fall, the man who, despising better things, has chosen worse. « He bought therefore, » etc. Allegorically the purchase of Joseph, who bought the land of Egypt with corn, signifies that Christ redeems the world by doctrine and by the price of his blood. And as to what he says: « Take seed, and sow the fields that you may have corn, » etc., the seed is the word of God, the fields are the bodies which, ploughed up by the evangelical share and sown with spiritual seed, bring forth the fruit of the virtues. The fifth part, which was given to the king, is the tribute of the five senses, which is to be paid to Christ the king. The four remaining are left to the possessors for seed and for food, because the use of the active life is granted to every one, that he may sow the works of the virtues and reap the fruit of justice unto life eternal, which is bestowed on us by Christ. Whence [0176C] it is said to Joseph: « Our salvation is in thy hand, » because the salvation of the world is in the power of Christ.

VERS. 22-28.-- « Except the land of the priests, » etc. This signifies that the land of the Church, in which are the true priests, is free from worldly tribute, which is fed unceasingly by the divine word, and shall suffer no loss of the spiritual bread.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « And when he saw that the day of his death drew near, » etc. [n: (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.)] He binds his son by that oath, etc., *as far as* who appeared in baptism about the thirtieth year.

VERS. 31.-- « He swearing. » [n: (AUG., ubi supra. )] That which the Latin codices have, « He adored upon the head of his rod, » some have more correctly: « He adored [0176D] upon the head of his own rod, » or « on the head of his own rod, » or « on the top, *or* upon the top of his own rod. » And therefore it is rightly asked what it is that has been said, etc., *as far as* where the figure of a thing to come was prefigured.

[n: (HIERON.)] « And Israel adored toward the top of his rod. » In this place also certain men vainly pretend that Jacob adored the top of Joseph's sceptre: namely, that honouring his son he adored his power, whereas in the Hebrew it is read far otherwise: « And Israel adored at the head of the bed; » because after his son had sworn to him, being secure of his request, he adored God, turned toward the head of the bed. For a holy man, and one given over to God, weighed down by old age, kept his little bed [0177A] so placed that the very posture of one lying down was, without any difficulty, ready for prayer.



## CHAPTER XLVIII.

VERS. 1.-- « These things being thus done . . . . taking his two sons Manasses and Ephraim. » [n: (HIERON.)] And he took his two sons with him, Manasses and Ephraim. From this appears what we said above, that Joseph up to that time had only two sons, Ephraim and Manasses. But if, after many years, when Jacob was dying, he brought only two to the blessing, then assuredly at that time, when his sons could not yet beget, being little ones and at the breast, at the coming in of his father and his brethren, he could not have grandsons of them.

VERS. 2-4.-- « Who being strengthened, » etc. [n: (HIERON.)] [0177B] And Israel being strengthened sat upon the bed. Why the Seventy have given out the same word now one way and now another I do not know, but I say boldly that the same word *mittha*, which here they translated *a bed*, above, where he said that Jacob adored, they named *a rod*.

VERS. 5, 6.-- « Two sons therefore who were born, » etc. [n: (HIERON., Quaest in Gen.)] It is plain that seventy souls went into Egypt, etc., *as far as* but they shall be mingled into the tribes of Ephraim and Manasses as appendages.

VERS. 7-11.-- « For when I came from Mesopotamia, Rachel died and I buried her, » etc. He points out to Joseph, as to one who did not know, where and when he had buried his mother, perhaps prophetically recalling that his mother was buried in the place where Christ was to be born.

[0177C]VERS. 12-15.-- « And when Joseph had taken them from his father's lap, » etc. [n: (Isid. in Gen., tom. V.)] As was done in Esau and Jacob, the Lord saying: « The elder shall serve the younger, » so also in the sons of Joseph, etc., *as far as* and the younger from the left hand to the right.

VERS. 16-21.-- « Let my name be called upon them. » [n: (AUG.)] It is to be noted from this place that not only a being heard, but an invoking also, is spoken of, which are not God's but men's.

VERS. 22.-- « I give thee one portion, » etc. [n: (HIERON. in Gen., tom. III.)] « I have given thee Sichem as a chief portion above thy brethren, which I received out of the hands of the Amorrhites with my sword and my arrow. »[var: Vulg. Gn 48:22 reads *Do tibi partem unam extra fratres tuos, quam tuli de manu Amorrhaei in gladio et arcu meo* — "I give thee one portion beyond thy brethren, which I took out of the hand of the Amorrhite with my sword and bow."] Sichima is declined [0177D] according to Greek and Latin usage, etc., *as far as* and his tomb is beheld there to this day.

[n: (AUG.)] As to Jacob's saying that he gives Sichem as a chief portion to his son Joseph, and his adding that he had possessed it with sword and bow, it is asked how this may agree to the letter with Gen. XXXIV. For he bought that possession with a hundred lambs, he did not take it by right of war. Or was it because his sons had stormed Salem, the city of the Sichemites, and it could become his by right of war, so that the war might seem just against those who first did the injury by defiling his daughter? Why then did he not give it to his elder sons, who did this? Then, if he now gives it to Joseph as glorying of the victory, why did the sons who committed it displease him at that time? Why, lastly, when he was blessing them, did he mention it by way of reproach? [0178A] There lies hidden here therefore a prophetic sacrament, because Joseph by a certain special signification signified Christ: and to him is given that land where Jacob buried the strange gods [n: (Gen. XXXV)], that Christ may be understood as about to possess the Gentiles who renounce the gods of their fathers and believe in Christ.



## CHAPTER XLIX.

[n: (ALBIN.)] In the blessings of Jacob the history is to be held and the allegory searched out: the history concerning the division of the land of promise, the allegory concerning Christ and the Church, which is to be revealed in the last times; yet first the foundations of the history must be laid.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « Ruben my firstborn, » etc. [n: (HIERON., Quaest. in Gen.)] « Ruben my firstborn [0178B], thou art my strength, » and the beginning of my sons,[var: Vulg. Gn 49:3 *et principium doloris mei*, "and the beginning of my sorrow."] etc., *as far as* thou hast lost the order of the firstborn.

VERS. 5.-- « Simeon and Levi, » etc. [n: (HIERON., ubi supra. )] LXX: « Simeon and Levi brethren have accomplished the iniquity of their own devising, » etc., *as far as* « let not my soul go into their counsel. »

VERS. 8.-- « Juda, they shall praise thee, » etc. [n: (HIERON., ubi supra. )] Because Juda is interpreted *confession* or *praise*, etc., *as far as* in other words the Church is shown according to the same sense, of which it is said, Matth. V: « A city cannot be hid, set upon a mountain. »

VERS. 9.-- « The whelp of a lion is Juda: to the prey, [0178C] my son, thou art gone up: resting thou hast couched as a lion, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] Or, the lion's whelp rested in sleep, because not of necessity but of power. Whence: « I have power to lay down my life, and no man taketh it from me, but I lay it down [n: (Joan. X)]. »[var: Vulg. Jo 10:18 *Nemo tollit eam a me : sed ego pono eam a meipso, et potestatem habeo ponendi eam*, "No man taketh it away from me: but I lay it down of myself, and I have power to lay it down."] But as to what he adds, « and as a lion's whelp, »[var: Vulg. Gn 49:9 *et quasi leaena*, "and as a lioness."] for thence he died whence he was born. Naturalists say that the whelp of a lion, when it has been born, sleeps three days and three nights. Then the place of its lair, shaken by the roaring of its father, rouses the sleeper: Christ also, lying three nights in the lair of the sepulchre, fulfilled the sleep of death. Well therefore did Christ rest as a lion, who overcame not only the bitterness of death but its dominion also, and as a lion's whelp, because on the third day he rose again. Whence it is added.

[0178D] « Who shall rouse him up? » etc. None, understand, save he himself who says, Joan. II: « Loose this temple, and in three days' space I will raise it again. »

VERS. 10.-- « The sceptre shall not be taken away from Juda, » etc. For so long were there kings of the seed of Juda among the Jews, until Christ should be born of the Virgin. For the first foreign king in the nation of the Jews was Herod, when Christ was already born. He therefore who denies that Christ has come, let him show a king of the tribe of Juda in the nation of the Jews.

VERS. 11.-- « Tying his colt to the vineyard, » etc. The Gentiles whom he joined to himself. Whence Ezech. XVIII: « As I live, saith the Lord, unless I put on these men as a garment. »[var: Vulg. Is 49:18, whose words these render, reads *Vivo ego, dicit Dominus, quia omnibus his velut ornamento vestieris*, "as I live, saith the Lord, thou shalt be clothed with all these as with an ornament"; Ez 18, the chapter Migne cites, has *si erit ultra vobis parabola haec in proverbium in Israel*.] For Christ cleansed us in the blood [0179A] of the grape when he hung like a cluster on the wood of the cross, and out of his side there went forth water for washing, and blood for redemption.

VERS. 12.-- « Fairer the eyes, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] Because their doctrine surpasses the austerity of the old law, and the evangelical precepts are clearer than the commandments of the law.

« With milk, » that is, the doctrine of the law, which fed the carnal or little people with milk: but « whiter, » the doctors of the Church, who eat and distribute the strong and solid food of the word. Whence Hebr. V: « Strong meat is for the perfect, » who are well called « whiter than milk, » because they are white, pure and free from every stain.

VERS. 13.-- « Zabulon on the shore, » etc. That he may show a refuge [0179B] to them that believe and a haven of faith to them that are in peril. Made firm against all the ends of the world, she awaits the shipwreck of the Jews and the storms of the heretics: who are carried about with every wind of doctrine, thence also were some of the apostles taken: there the Lord often taught. And he reaches as far as Sidon, that is, he comes as far as the Gentiles. Whence Isa. IX: « The land of Zabulon, and the land of Nephthali, the way of the sea beyond the Jordan, » etc.[var: Vulg. Is 9:1 *Primo tempore alleviata est terra Zabulon et terra Nephthali : et novissimo aggravata est via maris trans Jordanem Galilaeae gentium*, "at the first time the land of Zabulon and the land of Nephthali was lightened: and at the last the way of the sea beyond the Jordan of the Galilee of the Gentiles was heavily loaded"; the wording Migne prints is that of Mt 4:15.] Sidon, *hunting:* and out of those places were taken the hunters, of whom it is said Jer. XVI: « I will send many hunters, and they shall hunt you in every mountain. »[var: Vulg. Jr 16:16 *mittam eis multos venatores, et venabuntur eos de omni monte*, "I will send them many hunters, and they shall hunt them out of every mountain" — *eos*, "them," not *vos*, "you."]

VERS. 14, 15.-- « Issachar. » [n: (HIERON. Quaest. Hebr. )] He desired the good, resting in the midst of the clergy, etc., *as far as* and therefore let all the tribes serve him, [0179C] as bearing gifts to a master.

« Couching between the borders, » etc. To couch is to rest with the end of the world awaited, and to seek nothing of worldly things, but to long for the last things. The strong ass saw rest, and the best land: when the simple Gentile world raised itself up to the strength of the work, because it tends toward the country of life.

VERS. 16-18.-- « Dan shall judge his people, » etc. [n: (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.)] Others think that by these words Antichrist is foretold as to come of the tribe of Dan, etc., *as far as* and after the benefits of a feigned sweetness he plies the horns of power.

« Dan shall judge his people as one of the sceptres of Israel. Let Dan be a serpent in the way, a basilisk in [0179D] the path, biting the hoofs of the horse, and let his rider fall backward. I will look for thy Saviour, O Lord. »[var: Vulg. Gn 49:16-18 *sicut et alia tribus in Israel … cerastes in semita … ut cadat ascensor ejus retro. Salutare tuum expectabo, Domine*, "as another tribe in Israel … a horned serpent in the path … that his rider may fall backward. I will look for thy salvation, O Lord."] [n: (HIERON., ubi supra. )] Samson, the judge of Israel, was of the tribe of Dan, etc., *as far as* and he himself shall be the expectation of the nations.

VERS. 19, 20.-- « Gad. » [n: (HIERON.)] A robber shall rob before him,[var: Vulg. Gn 49:19 *Gad, accinctus praeliabatur ante eum*, "Gad, being girded, fought before him."] etc., *as far as* and, his enemies overcome, he fought bravely.

« Gad girded shall fight before him. » [n: (ISID.)] Christ shall fight before and behind against Antichrist: before, by the hidden coming of his humility; after, by the manifested glory of his majesty. Whence Moses, Deut. XXXIII: « Blessed in breadth is Gad, he hath rested as a lion: and he took the arm and the crown, and he saw his principality. » For Christ rested in the sepulchre, and humbled the crown [0180A] and pride of the lofty, and to him was given the principality, and honour, and a kingdom.

VERS. 21.-- « Nephthali. » [n: (HIERON.)] *Allegorically.* A shoot let loose, giving beauty to the nation,[var: Vulg. Gn 49:21 *Nephthali, cervus emissus, et dans eloquia pulchritudinis*, "Nephthali, a hart let loose, and giving words of beauty."] etc., *as far as* because there most of all he taught, as is written in the Gospel too.

« Nephthali a hart let loose, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] For the apostles and preachers, leaping forth swiftly like harts, overleap the hindrances of the world, and meditating on lofty things, give words of beauty, that is, they preach the doctrine of the Saviour.

VERS. 22-26.-- « Joseph a growing son, » etc. [n: (HIERON., ubi supra. )] « A son growing above a fountain, »[var: Vulg. Gn 49:22 *filius accrescens et decorus aspectu*, "a growing son and comely to behold."] etc., *as far as* ruling also over the ten tribes of Israel.

[0180B]VERS. 27. « Benjamin a ravening wolf, » etc. [n: (HIERON., ibid. )] « In the morning he shall eat the prey, and at evening he shall divide the food. »[var: Vulg. Gn 49:27 *mane comedat praedam, et vespere dividet spolia*, "in the morning let him eat the prey, and in the evening he shall divide the spoil."] Although the prophecy is most manifestly of Paul, in that in his youth he persecuted the Church, and in his old age was a preacher of the Gospel, etc., *as far as* concerning the priests who, serving the altar, should live by the altar [n: (I Cor. IX)].

« Benjamin a ravening wolf, » etc. [n: (ISID.)] A certain one of the doctors referred this to Jerusalem, etc., *as far as* then shall he divide the food, understanding that the letter is to be divided from the spirit, because « the letter killeth, but the spirit quickeneth [n: (II Cor. III)]. »

VERS. 28-31.-- « All these in the tribes, » etc. It is asked: why did Jacob appoint all whom he begot of free women and of handmaids [0180C] sons and heirs with equal honour? because, as Christ bestows heavenly rewards with like honour and glory upon all who are joined to his body through faith. « For there is no distinction there whether one be Jew or Greek, Barbarian or Scythian, bond or free: because through all things and in all things » is Christ,[var: Vulg. Col 3:11 *ubi non est gentilis et Judaeus, circumcisio et praeputium, Barbarus et Scytha, servus et liber : sed omnia, et in omnibus Christus*, "where there is neither Gentile nor Jew, circumcision nor uncircumcision, Barbarian nor Scythian, bond nor free: but Christ is all, and in all."] who put on the form of a servant, and served both for the free man and for the servant, that he might bestow an equal gift upon all. For whosoever earns the faith of the Lord is darkened by no stains of carnal birth. Whence Joel. II: « I will pour forth of my spirit upon all flesh. »[var: Vulg. Joel 2:28 *effundam spiritum meum super omnem carnem*, "I will pour out my spirit upon all flesh"; the wording Migne prints is that of Act 2:17.]

VERS. 32.-- « And being gathered, » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] It must be asked: of what people is this said? For concerning the fathers there is no question. For it is known that he was gathered to Abraham and Isaac, but a people is a multitude. [0180D] Those, however, being so few, could not be called a people. It is to be understood therefore that this is said of the angels also, to whose fellowship the saints, passing out of the world, are gathered. Whence Hebr. XII: « You are come to mount Sion, and to the heavenly Jerusalem, and to the company of angels, »[var: Vulg. Hbr 12:22 *accessistis ad Sion montem, et civitatem Dei viventis, Jerusalem caelestem, et multorum millium angelorum frequentiam*, "you are come to mount Sion, and to the city of the living God, the heavenly Jerusalem, and to the company of many thousands of angels."] etc.



## CHAPTER L.

VERS. 1-3.-- « Which when Joseph saw, he fell . . . . forty days passed. » [n: (STRAB.)] Which number befits penance and humility. Whence in the law and in the Gospel it was observed by the prophets and by the Lord himself.

[n: (AUG.)] The forty days of burial which the Scripture records perhaps signify the penance whereby sins are buried. For not in vain were forty days [0181A] of fastings appointed, on which Moses, and Elias, and the Lord himself fasted, and the Church calls the chief observance of fastings Lent. Whence also concerning the Ninivites it is written in Jonas: « Yet forty days, and Ninive shall be destroyed [n: (Jon. III)], that through so many days, fitted, that is, to the humbling of the penitent, they may be understood to have wept for their own sins in fastings, and to have obtained mercy. Nor does this number befit the mourning of penitents only. For forty days after the resurrection the Lord was with his disciples, going in and going out, eating and drinking, and those were days of gladness. Nor are the seventy Interpreters to be believed to have erred, who said: « Three days, and Ninive shall be destroyed: » for, endowed with a greater authority than the office of interpreters is, by the prophetic spirit (by which also they are affirmed to have agreed with one mouth in their interpretations, which was a great miracle) they set down [0181B] « three days, » although they were not ignorant that « forty days » was read in the Hebrew; that in the glorification of Christ sins might be understood to be dissolved and abolished, « who was delivered up for our offences, and rose again for our justification. » But the glorification of the Lord is known in his resurrection and ascension. Whence here, although he gave one and the same Spirit: first after he rose again, secondly after he ascended; and because after three days he rose again, but after forty days ascended: the one of these, which was done later, the Hebrews signify by the number of forty days; but the other, of the three days, which [0181C] belongs to the same matter, the Seventy record, not by the servitude of interpretation, but by the authority of prophecy. Let us not therefore say that one of these is false, and let us not wrangle for some interpreters against others; since they who interpret out of the Hebrew prove that what they interpret is written; and the authority of the Seventy, which is commended by so great a miracle divinely wrought, is confirmed by so great an antiquity in the churches.

« Seventy days. » [n: (STRAB.)] The heathen bewailed their dead nine days. Whence also the ninth day was called the novendial, when they were buried. The worshippers of God celebrated the obsequies for seven days. Whence Eccli. XXII: « The mourning for the dead is seven days: but the mourning of a fool is all the time of his life. »[var: Vulg. Sir 22:13 *Luctus mortui septem dies : fatui autem et impii omnes dies vitae illorum*, "the mourning for the dead is seven days: but for the fool and the ungodly, all the days of their life."] For the number seven signifies rest. Whence the seventh day is called the sabbath, [0181D] that is, *rest*. They celebrate therefore the seventh day of the dead, wishing that they may rest with the Lord from the labours of the present life. Hence therefore it is said: « Egypt wept for him seventy days, » which is the sevenfold number multiplied by ten. For seven times ten are seventy. It may also be said that, as the third day of a dead man is kept by the faithful, that the soul, which is of a threefold nature, may rest; so the seventh day is celebrated, that the soul, which sinned at the enticement of the flesh, may be reconciled to the body. In the number seven also there are three and [0182A] four; the number three belongs to the soul, which is irascible, and concupiscible, and rational; the number four to the body, which consists of four elements.

VERS. 4-21.-- « And when the time of the mourning was expired, » etc. [n: (AUG.)] As to what Joseph enjoined upon the great men of Egypt, that they should say to Pharao in his name: « My father made me swear, saying: In the monument which I made for myself in the land of Chanaan, there thou shalt bury me; »[var: Vulg. Gn 50:5 *eo quod pater meus adjuraverit me dicens : En morior : in sepulchro meo, quod fodi mihi in terra Chanaan, sepelies me*, "for that my father made me swear, saying: Behold I die: thou shalt bury me in my sepulchre which I dug for myself in the land of Chanaan" — *fodi*, "dug," which is the very word the gloss below reasons from, where Migne's lemma prints *feci*, "made."] it is asked how this may be true, since these words of his father, when he gave charge concerning his burial, are not read. But the words are to be referred to the sense, as other things above were likewise repeated. For words must serve a will that is to be uttered and brought to knowledge. But that Jacob dug himself a sepulchre is nowhere read above, yet assuredly, unless he had dug it, it would not now be said.

[0182B]VERS. 10.-- « And they came to the threshing-floor of Atad, » etc. This place is beyond the Jordan, and is distant from Hebron seventy thousand paces. They passed by, therefore, the place in which they were to bury him, and afterwards, the Jordan being crossed, returned to the place of the double cave. Whence it may be asked why they did this. If any one says that they feared wars, as the children of Israel were led about through the desert for forty years, until, the Jordan being passed, they came to the land of promise. But this was done for the sake of a greater signification. For in the place which is called the threshing-floor of Arad, afterwards the multitude of the children of Israel, murmuring against the Lord, was consumed by divine fire. With so great a mourning, therefore, not so much the death of Jacob as the coming ruin of the people was bewailed.

VERS. 22, 23.-- « And he lived a hundred and ten years. » [0182C] [n: (AUG., in Gen., tom. III.)] Since the Scripture says that Joseph, while he lived, saw these sons of his sons or grandsons of his sons, how does it join them to the sixty-five souls with whom it says that Jacob entered Egypt, etc., *as far as* which was propagated while he lived, through whom it came about that he entered in.

VERS. 24.-- « Carry away my bones, » etc. [n: (STRAB.)] Joseph, like his father, desired with his whole mind the land of promise, and detested the sojourn in Egypt. He instructs us each morally also, that so long as we are in this world we should always desire the land of promise, that is, of the living, and should wish to rest there: which is well done, if we imitate in our manners the number of Joseph's years. For he died when a hundred and ten years of his life were completed: let us also study [0182D] to come, through the keeping of the decalogue, to the eternal blessedness which the number one hundred signifies.

VERS. 25.-- « Embalmed with spices. » [n: (STRAB.)] Happy is the soul which, seasoned with the spices of the virtues while it dwells in the little coffer of the body, is kept by daily profiting for the everlasting life. For if it shall have been guarded in faith, hope and charity, it will deserve to come, by the grace of Christ, to the sight of the divine contemplation, whose adoption is perfect gladness; whence psal. XV: « Thou shalt fill me with joy with thy countenance. »
